## DEC-20260811-001 - Evidence-first workspace

- **Context:** Long-running work must survive agent changes and distinguish evidence from interpretation.
- **Decision:** Keep one Git repository with `Vault/` as durable shared memory; use small evidence IDs and reviewable static/runtime exports.
- **Alternatives:** Conversation-only memory; a separate vault repository; a vector database at bootstrap.
- **Reasoning:** Plain Markdown, structured properties, Git, `rg`, `fzf`, and `jq` are inspectable and sufficient for initial scale.
- **Consequences:** Meaningful sessions must update the vault; generated binary/tool state remains excluded.
- **Revisit when:** Repository search measurably fails on a documented research task.

## DEC-20260811-002 - Environment responsibilities

- **Context:** Period accuracy, iteration speed, and dynamic debugging require different tradeoffs.
- **Decision:** Use 86Box/Win98 as reference, Wine for fast tests, KVM Windows for optional debugging, and PCem only for cross-validation.
- **Alternatives:** Treat Wine as reference; build duplicate emulator environments immediately.
- **Reasoning:** Separating roles makes deviations visible and avoids overbuilding.
- **Consequences:** Important behavioral claims should identify the environment and seek cross-environment evidence.
- **Revisit when:** Actual game behavior demonstrates a blocking incompatibility.

## DEC-20260811-003 - Quarantine cross-port evidence

- **Context:** The PS1 reverse-engineering project may contain useful analogues, but it is a separate platform repository with extensive binary and generated working artifacts.
- **Decision:** Keep it external and read-only. Cite bounded evidence by source ID, commit, path, and locator. Allow PS1 evidence to seed PC hypotheses, never to confirm them.
- **Alternatives:** Copy its vault or exports; vendor it; add a submodule; ignore the PS1 work entirely.
- **Reasoning:** An external provenance boundary preserves ABLPC search quality and Git history while retaining legitimate comparative value.
- **Consequences:** Cross-port work requires a comparison note and independent PC confirmation. No PS1 artifact tree enters ABLPC.
- **Revisit when:** A reproducible build or machine-readable export API creates a concrete need that citations cannot satisfy.

## DEC-20260811-004 - Wine-first modding target

- **Context:** The researcher primarily intends to play and test on Linux, while the Windows 98 reference environment is blocked on user-owned media.
- **Decision:** Treat the unchanged PC executable under recorded Wine configurations as the primary supported modding runtime. Retain Windows 98 as a non-blocking historical cross-check for compatibility-sensitive questions.
- **Alternatives:** Block releases on Windows 98 confirmation; remove Windows 98 validation from the project entirely.
- **Reasoning:** Wine already reaches a user-confirmed working state and supports the intended user environment. Keeping Windows 98 optional preserves historical validation without stopping PC-native research or tools.
- **Consequences:** Wine-scoped releases must say so explicitly. Untested Windows 98 behavior remains unknown rather than implied compatible.
- **Revisit when:** A Wine-specific deviation prevents a mod or a period-correct behavior is required to discriminate competing interpretations.

## DEC-20260812-005 - Hardware-accelerated aspect-preserving Linux play baseline

- **Context:** The opening selector defaults to a 320×200 software renderer, which remained small inside a 640×480 nested surface. The user primarily plays on a 2560×1440 Linux host and does not want the 4:3 image stretched.
- **Decision:** Use the game's hardware-acceleration option as the primary play and visual-research renderer. Present its logged 640×480×16 Direct3D output through Gamescope with the `fit` scaler. Default to nearest-neighbor filtering while exposing linear filtering as a non-authoritative presentation preference.
- **Alternatives:** Use the 320×200 software renderer; stretch to 16:9; depend on Wine's unmanaged fullscreen behavior; treat Windows 98 as a prerequisite.
- **Reasoning:** `EXP-20260812-007` records the renderer's actual mode and an approximately 1920×1440 centered 4:3 compositor presentation on the tested host. This supports the intended Linux workflow without claiming historical equivalence.
- **Consequences:** The launcher instructs the user to choose the second renderer option because no persistent selector setting is yet confirmed. Software remains available as a diagnostic cross-check. Online video is not a fidelity reference without known source and capture settings.
- **Revisit when:** A reliable persistent renderer setting is found, the host display changes, or controlled Windows 98 evidence establishes a different authoritative presentation configuration.

## DEC-20260812-006 - Natural-pause hardware watchpoints for Wine WoW64

- **Context:** WineDbg's GDB proxy faulted in an injected thread, and native GDB could hit a software breakpoint in the 32-bit executable but faulted when continuing amid an architecture mismatch warning. Native GDB hardware read watchpoints installed at the game's natural Training title pause successfully captured the intended reads and allowed gameplay to continue.
- **Decision:** For bounded read-tracing on the current Wine 11 WoW64 target, prefer native GDB hardware watchpoints installed while the game is naturally paused. Restart the target after any WineDbg proxy attempt. Treat software breakpoint use as unsupported until a controlled fixture or configuration demonstrates stable continuation.
- **Alternatives:** Continue through WineDbg's GDB proxy; depend on native software breakpoints; require a Windows debugging VM before further dynamic research.
- **Reasoning:** `RUN-GDB-20260812-021920` captured all three intended reads at `0x0044D5B8` and then reached `NextLife`. The natural pause removes the need to alter code or timing to install the watchpoints.
- **Consequences:** This is a narrow Wine research method, not an assertion about Windows behavior or general GDB compatibility. Negative debugger faults must remain separate from game defects.
- **Revisit when:** A debugger with explicit stable WoW64 support is installed, a Windows debugging VM becomes available, or the next bounded target cannot be reached through a natural pause and hardware watchpoint.

## DEC-20260812-007 - Evidence-gated canonical roadmap

- **Context:** The charter defines broad research phases and the state/unknown registers describe the present evidence, but the project lacked one durable sequence from current findings to practical modding. Ad hoc task selection risks broad decompilation, duplicated plans, and premature implementation.
- **Decision:** Use [[Project Roadmap]] as the single canonical prioritization layer. Keep one active milestone and one exact next work packet. Advance milestones through cited exit gates rather than percentages or calendar estimates.
- **Alternatives:** Continue selecting tasks only from Current State; maintain separate research and implementation roadmaps; build a comprehensive function-by-function checklist; use a date-based release plan before scope is known.
- **Reasoning:** One evidence-gated roadmap connects research to user-visible mod capability while preserving the established separation between mission, facts, unknowns, indexes, and chronology.
- **Consequences:** [[Current State]] names the active packet, [[Known Unknowns]] records uncertainty, MOCs index evidence, and sessions record history. These notes link to the roadmap rather than becoming competing plans. Roadmap changes that alter scope or strategy require a decision entry.
- **Revisit when:** The project supports multiple independent contributors whose work cannot be represented by one active milestone, or when release commitments require a separate dated delivery plan.

## DEC-20260812-008 - Positive-count dispatch-0x49 target tooling boundary

- **Context:** `EXP-20260812-009` bounds one payload-target layout through the `0x49` scene dispatch and `0x0045A7F0`, but the consumer also has a distinct negative-count branch and other scene dispatches use other payload paths.
- **Decision:** Expose read-only target inspection and guarded target rebinding only when `(flags & 0xBFEF) == 0x49`, both old and new targets pass the positive-count block parser, the manifest matches the exact source hash, and the expected old target matches. Do not expose arbitrary byte patches or negative-count target editing.
- **Alternatives:** Parse the entire suffix as one format; accept any in-range target offset; add a generic byte-patch manifest operation; delay all target tooling until every dispatch is understood.
- **Reasoning:** The narrow boundary is supported by exact PC code and 990 cross-file records, enables a discriminating M1.2 experiment, and keeps unsupported layouts visible.
- **Consequences:** Reciprocal Training target candidates can be staged reproducibly, but they remain experimental until original-executable runs demonstrate their effects. Unsupported dispatches fail explicitly.
- **Revisit when:** PC evidence bounds the negative-count branch or another dispatch class, or the guarded rebind experiment contradicts the current consumer model.

## DEC-20260812-009 - Hardware renderer evidence gate

- **Context:** The preliminary `EXP-20260812-010` selector process entered
  `INITSOFTRENDER` at 320×200, while the accepted process entered the Direct3D
  renderer at 640×480×16. An intended Down input was not sufficient proof and
  the software run had to be rejected. Later F0-001/F0-002 results further
  reject treating one Down or an assumed default as deterministic.
- **Decision:** Renderer-facing RE uses the second, hardware-acceleration
  option unless the bounded question explicitly compares renderers. Accept a
  hardware run only when its game log contains `Screen mode 640x480x16
  selected` and `Starting Direct3D renderer`, and does not enter
  `INITSOFTRENDER`.
- **Alternatives:** Treat software and hardware output as interchangeable;
  trust intended UI input; infer the path from screenshot dimensions alone.
- **Reasoning:** The two selections initialize materially different renderer,
  surface, texture, and submission paths. Log markers prove which code path
  produced the evidence.
- **Consequences:** [[START HERE]], [[Agent Operating Manual]], and
  [[Wine Linux Play Baseline]] carry the same gate. Software remains available
  as an explicitly labeled comparison, never as an implicit hardware control.
- **Revisit when:** A hash-verified persistent renderer setting or automated
  launcher can prove the hardware selection before game initialization.

## DEC-20260812-010 - Offset-named signed +0x0C editing boundary

- **Context:** `EXP-20260812-011` corrects the first three scene-record dwords
  from presumed render position to dispatch-`0x49` candidate center.
  `EXP-20260812-012` proves a visibility-bound use for one positive `+0x0C`
  value, but 508 records in the local corpus have negative signed values whose
  convention is unknown.
- **Decision:** Retire current `position`/`angle` claims from format dossiers.
  Expose the signed word only as expected-value-guarded
  `set-scene-record-field-0c`; do not give the manifest operation a radius,
  angle, or extent name until the signed variants are discriminated.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the prior angle label; name it radius from positive
  math alone; expose a generic record-byte patch; delay the bounded positive
  experiment until all signed cases are understood.
- **Reasoning:** Offset naming preserves the confirmed edit capability without
  turning the still-unknown sign convention into an API promise.
- **Consequences:** The parser changes only the selected two bytes, requires
  the expected original signed value, and retains unknown fields. Current docs
  may describe positive visibility-bound behavior while labeling complete
  semantics unknown.
- **Revisit when:** PC-native static/runtime evidence discriminates negative
  `+0x0C` values across representative records or a versioned schema migration
  formalizes a stronger field name.

## DEC-20260812-011 - Keep the DAT resource table separate from the M1 placement bridge

- **Context:** `EXP-20260812-016` binds Training plant IDs to exact bytes in a
  type-`0x3F` top record of the same `LEVEL1.DAT` processed by the M1 fixup, but
  those bytes are before and disjoint from the M1 20-byte scene records,
  payloads, and positive targets.
- **Decision:** Call this a same-DAT separate-table dependency. Do not promote
  it to an M1 placement-record-to-resource bridge. Begin the bounded `.ANM`
  inspector from its independently evidenced loader and size boundary while
  retaining the stronger placement identity as an explicit M2 exit-gate
  unknown.
- **Alternatives:** Treat any shared DAT/fixup path as the M1 bridge; block all
  resource-format inspection until a placement record is bound; discard the
  separate-table result as irrelevant.
- **Reasoning:** The exact producer is useful and independently corroborated,
  but range identity matters. `.ANM` already satisfies the roadmap's parser
  entry gate through its exact loader-size and relocation walk.
- **Consequences:** M2.3 closes honestly, M2.4 can proceed, and `KU-023`
  prevents the milestone exit from silently losing the missing placement
  dependency.
- **Revisit when:** PC evidence binds an M1 20-byte record or positive target
  to a resource identity, or `.ANM` inspection contradicts the current loader
  boundary.

## DEC-20260812-012 - Keep ANM tooling read-only at the first-consumer boundary

- **Context:** `EXP-20260812-017` exactly reproduces the first-level `.ANM`
  reads at `0x00444B70`, but most nested fields remain unknown, the original
  consumer has no file-size argument, and the caller patches two header words
  before use.
- **Decision:** Expose only lossless read-only inspection and fail-closed size,
  count, and offset validation. Do not expose relocation writing, nested field
  edits, or semantic names. Test M2.5 with a whole-file copy between exact
  same-size/same-first-level-layout resources already present in the user's
  work tree.
- **Alternatives:** Add a generic `.ANM` byte editor; label nested values from
  shape alone; block all replacement work until the complete format is known.
- **Reasoning:** Read-only tooling is fully supported and useful now. A
  hash-guarded whole-file replacement can discriminate resource contribution
  without pretending an unknown field is understood or distributing assets.
- **Consequences:** `inspect-anm` and `scan-anm` report metadata only. M2.5
  leaves `.ALL` unchanged so acceptance, rejection, and visible effect each
  discriminate the current pair-contract hypotheses.
- **Revisit when:** PC runtime/static evidence establishes a writable field
  contract or the whole-file replacement exposes a stricter compatibility
  boundary.

## DEC-20260812-013 - Do not promote accepted-no-visible ANM replacement as a mod class

- **Context:** `EXP-20260812-018` confirms original-executable acceptance of
  one exact same-size/same-first-level-layout `.ANM` substitution with the
  destination `.ALL` unchanged, but finds no stable visible difference at the
  tested Training start camera beyond control-to-control animation variance.
- **Decision:** Retain the read-only ANM CLI boundary and classify the external
  whole-file copy only as an experiment recipe. Do not publish a generic ANM
  replacement manifest or call this a useful asset mod. Use an exact same-size
  paired `.ANM`/`.ALL` substitution as the next discriminator, without adding
  `.ALL` parsing or semantic field names.
- **Alternatives:** Promote any accepted ANM copy as a supported mod; declare
  `.ANM` irrelevant from one no-visible camera; begin editing unknown `.ALL`
  fields; abandon the plant family after a negative visual result.
- **Reasoning:** Acceptance establishes a compatibility fact but not user value
  or semantics. The independently coordinated `.ALL` remains the most bounded
  untested component, and plant 04/10 provide an equal-size exact source pair.
- **Consequences:** M2.5 closes honestly as accepted-no-visible-change at one
  camera. M2.6 remains a staged user-owned whole-pair experiment; no game bytes
  or public resource edit surface enters Git.
- **Revisit when:** A paired replacement or a later PC-native consumer trace
  establishes a stable visible/behavioral role and a safe authoring boundary.

## DEC-20260812-014 - Pivot from blind pair swaps to request suppression

- **Context:** `EXP-20260812-019` confirms that the exact equal-size plant 10
  `.ANM`/`.ALL` pair is accepted at the plant 04 paths, but—like the M2.5
  ANM-only arm—produces no stable Training start-camera difference beyond
  independent control animation variance.
- **Decision:** Do not promote the pair copy as a visible asset mod, generic
  resource replacement, or `.ALL` compatibility rule. Use the already bound
  Training selector at DAT offset `0x394` for the next discriminator: suppress
  only the plant 04 request, first require the predicted load-log omission,
  then compare any stable visual mask with independently retained M1 masks.
- **Alternatives:** Publish the accepted whole-pair copy as a mod class; test
  `.ALL`-only replacement despite the whole pair having no stable visual
  result; continue swapping unrelated plant pairs; begin semantic `.ALL`
  parsing from equal size and acceptance.
- **Reasoning:** Request suppression has a strong PC-native log prediction and
  directly tests whether plant 04 participates in the rendered state. It can
  also connect the separate DAT resource table toward the unresolved M1
  placement bridge without assigning unknown file fields.
- **Consequences:** M2.6 closes as an exact acceptance/no-visible-start result.
  M2.7 remains an ignored, one-byte, expected-value-guarded experiment; no
  selector editor, `.ALL` parser, game bytes, or screenshots enter Git.
- **Revisit when:** Request suppression contradicts the predicted load path,
  exposes a stable M1-correlated visual mask, or later-camera evidence makes a
  whole-pair substitution visibly discriminating.

## DEC-20260812-015 - Preserve selector-cursor coupling and target plant 10

- **Context:** The M2.7 plan assumed Training DAT `0x394` independently
  selected plant 04. Pre-staging review of exact instructions
  `0x004161EA–0x004161FA` showed that successive `0x2B` selectors consume a
  shared class-specific cursor. Removing `0x394` would leave ID 4 for the next
  `0x2B` at `0x3A4`, suppressing plant 10 rather than plant 04.
- **Decision:** Do not run or describe `0x394 → 0x00` as plant-04
  suppression. Preserve the contradiction and execute the clean one-byte
  `0x3A4: 0x2B → 0x00` plant-10 discriminator. Treat per-offset selector
  results as sequence-dependent wherever a class cursor is involved.
- **Alternatives:** Run the original packet and mislabel its predicted result;
  patch an executable constant to suppress plant 04; promote a multi-byte DAT
  recipe without an established safety boundary; return to blind file swaps.
- **Reasoning:** The second-selector edit has an exact static prediction and
  isolates one request without shifting a later `0x2B`. It stays within the
  authorized ignored level-data work copy and directly tests the existing
  PC-native dependency chain.
- **Consequences:** `EXP-20260812-020` confirms exact plant-10 request omission
  and `NextLife` acceptance, but finds no stable start-camera mask beyond
  independent control variance. Plant-04 suppression remains untested; no
  selector editor or public mod recipe is promoted. M2.8 moves to runtime
  consumer/access evidence rather than another wall-clock visual comparison.
- **Revisit when:** A guarded PC-native method can isolate plant 04 without
  cursor shifting, or plant 10's runtime destination can be connected to an
  M1 consumer with exact access evidence.

## DEC-20260812-016 - Move the consumer trace to the late-initialization boundary

- **Context:** `EXP-20260812-022` kept hardware read watchpoints on the two
  exact plant 10 entry `+0x20` dwords and adjusted first-entry dword from the
  natural title start through `NextLife` and 71.708 seconds of gameplay. None
  fired, although the values and Direct3D run gates remained exact.
- **Decision:** Treat the post-title pointer surface as a bounded negative, not
  as proof of irrelevance. Move the next trace boundary earlier: arm the same
  watches immediately after the caller adjustments at `0x00445B01`, and use
  resource-batch return `0x004679C7` plus the natural title as explicit bounds.
- **Alternatives:** Extend an unbounded idle gameplay watch; infer that plant
  10 is unused; return to visual pair swaps; widen immediately to every unknown
  entry field or begin `.ALL` semantic parsing.
- **Reasoning:** A late-initialization read/copy directly explains how source
  entry pointers could matter without later CPU rereads. The two explicit
  boundaries can discriminate that route with the same exact PC-native values
  before any broader field search.
- **Consequences:** M2.9 closes as a precise negative. M2.10 tests only the
  omitted `0x00445B01`-to-title interval and retains address-based names. No
  resource editor, placement identity, or cross-port claim is promoted.
- **Revisit when:** The late-initialization watches identify a reader/copy, or
  both explicit boundaries win without a read and justify widening to another
  statically evidenced entry field.

## DEC-20260812-017 - Guard the common resource-loop tail with a unique caller

- **Context:** The first M2.10 hardware stop at `0x00445B01` found all plant 10
  destinations zero. A later temporary watch was reported amid exact
  `0x00448010` population writes. Static instructions confirm `0x00445B01` is
  the common loop tail for multiple resource IDs, not a plant-10-unique point.
- **Decision:** Any plant-10-specific experiment beginning at `0x00445B01`
  must first stop at unique plant 10 post-call `0x0044596D`, verify resource ID
  `0x71` and indices `66/68`, and accept only the immediately following common-
  tail hit. Read watches begin only after the final target writes are stable.
- **Alternatives:** Treat the first common-tail stop as plant 10; infer a
  consumer from a watch reported during population; restart without recording
  the contradiction; use path strings alone without a live guard.
- **Reasoning:** The two-address sequence binds the shared control-flow point
  to exact plant 10 runtime state and separates initialization writes from
  downstream reads without changing memory or code.
- **Consequences:** `EXP-20260812-023` explicitly rejects the unguarded window
  and retains only the corrected two-boundary negative. M2.11 inherits the
  guard before widening to entry-head fields.
- **Revisit when:** A different resource has its own unique post-call address,
  or a future trace uses a distinct live discriminator that proves the same
  resource identity before the common tail.

## DEC-20260812-018 - Follow the +0x2E gates before widening entry fields

- **Context:** `EXP-20260812-025` finds no read of either exact nonzero plant
  10 entry `+0x0C` dword through guarded batch return or an unattended title.
  The two static `+0x0C` candidates at `0x00446A97/0x00447668` first compare
  selected entry word `+0x2E` against 9 or `0xA`.
- **Decision:** Trace only exact plant 10 words `0x005AAB2E` and
  `0x005AAB7E` next. Retain offset names and the same guard/return/title
  bounds; do not broaden to unrelated fields or assign state semantics.
- **Alternatives:** Extend the `+0x0C` watches through unbounded gameplay;
  infer the static candidates never consume plant 10; watch an arbitrary
  later field; begin `.ALL` semantic parsing.
- **Reasoning:** The gate words are populated by exact instructions in
  `0x00448010` and are read immediately before both candidate accesses. They
  can discriminate whether `0x00446970` selects plant 10 more directly than a
  broad entry sweep.
- **Consequences:** M2.12 closes as a bounded negative. M2.13 watches two
  exact words in one unchanged run; no resource editor or semantic field name
  is promoted.
- **Revisit when:** A `+0x2E` watch identifies a reader/state transition, or
  both exact words remain unread through the retained bounds.

## DEC-20260812-019 - Scripted read-only runtime tooling uplift

- **Context:** Runtime evidence is gathered by hand at its most expensive
  points. The GDB natural-pause hardware-watchpoint method
  (`DEC-20260812-006`) is manually driven and hand-transcribed, the 4-slot
  hardware-watchpoint budget and short manual windows produce the
  M2.8–M2.13 sequence of bounded negatives, and the free-running 15-frame
  comparison method fights animation noise. A survey of the researcher's
  DuckStation `external-debugger-control` fork supplies transferable tooling
  design patterns; it is recorded in
  [[External Source Registry#SRC-DUCKSTATION-DBG]].
- **Decision:** Add a bounded suite of scripted, read-only runtime-evidence
  tools under `scripts/` documented in [[Runtime Tooling]]: a GDB
  hardware-watchpoint harness, an `rr` record wrapper, `wine-capture.sh` relay
  and file-trace extensions, a frozen-frame capture, a `gcore` snapshot plus a
  read-only core scanner, and an `apitrace` GL arm. All debugger automation is
  read-only by construction — the harness exposes no memory/register/code/
  execution write verbs (`write_verbs: []`) — and the only privileged action
  is installing and deleting its own hardware watch/breakpoints at a natural
  pause, exactly the surface approved in `DEC-20260812-006`. Adopt the survey's
  patterns: structured completion results, machine-readable `--capabilities`,
  additive-only metadata schemas, and ownership proof (`/proc` identity) before
  any signal or attach. This tooling stream is bounded parallel work and does
  not displace the active M2 packet.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the fully manual method; expose memory/register write
  verbs now; stand up a localhost JSON-RPC/MCP control service in this
  iteration; adopt `rr` without a validation experiment.
- **Reasoning:** The uplift automates the demonstrated method rather than
  changing its authority, removes hand-transcription error, and makes bounded
  windows reproducible from a spec. `rr` (unlimited replay watchpoints,
  reverse execution) targets exactly the reader-detection questions the M2
  grind is starving for, but only as a distinct environment arm: recording
  forces `LIBGL_ALWAYS_SOFTWARE=1` (llvmpipe) because `rr` cannot record
  GPU-written shared memory, so it is valid for memory questions and not for
  pixel-fidelity questions. No MCP server is added; the README bootstrap
  excludes arbitrary RE MCP servers, and a control service would need its own
  decision.
- **Consequences:** New scripts follow the house pattern (refuse `originals/`,
  `ABLPC_ROOT`-overridable `RUN-<KIND>-<stamp>` provenance under
  `dynamic/wine/`, hash-gated, synthetic-fixture tests). Game-byte artifacts
  (cores, frames, `rr`/`apitrace` traces, raw transcripts) live only under
  git-ignored `work/` paths verified with `git check-ignore`; committed
  metadata carries hashes only. `rr` recording additionally requires
  `kernel.perf_event_paranoid <= 1`, recorded as a pending user `sysctl` in
  [[Environment Status]]. Live-game validation of every tool is staged as
  ready-to-run procedures in [[Runtime Tooling]], not executed in this packet.
- **Revisit when:** A staged validation contradicts a tool's assumptions
  (`rr` fails to replay a Wine WoW64 run, frozen-frame `SIGSTOP` perturbs
  later state), or a localhost control service becomes worth its own decision.

## DEC-20260812-020 - Runtime tooling live-validation outcomes

- **Context:** `DEC-20260812-019` staged live validation of the six runtime
  tools. All six were then run against the live Training game on the Fedora 44
  host. The record is in [[Runtime Tooling#Live validation results
  (2026-08-12)]]; the game-byte artifacts stayed under git-ignored `work/`.
- **Correction:** This entry originally attributed the `rr` block to an
  "NVIDIA ioctl." Follow-up research disproved that: the blocker is Wine 11's
  in-kernel `ntsync` synchronization driver (`ioctl(0x80044e88)` on
  `anon_inode:ntsync`), which `rr` 5.9.0 does not model. The claims below are
  corrected accordingly (`KU-028`, [[Runtime Tooling#The ntsync blocker for
  rr]]).
- **Decision:** Treat the six tools as live-validated at the demonstrated
  boundary. Adopt `gamescopectl screenshot <path>` (asynchronous; wait for the
  file) as the established frozen-capture backend, and `--api egl`
  (`egltrace.so`) as the apitrace arm for this WineD3D-over-EGL host. Record
  `rr` recording as environment-blocked here: it FATAL-aborts on Wine's
  unmodelled `ntsync` ioctl during Wine init (`RUN-RR-20260812-183423`), while
  `rr` works on non-Wine programs and the `rr-record.sh` wrapper is validated.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the tools staged/unvalidated; hardcode a different
  capture backend; treat the `rr` failure as a wrapper bug; abandon the `rr`
  arm entirely.
- **Reasoning:** Validation confirmed the tools and surfaced three real bugs
  that synthetic fixtures missed (Windows-path cmdline basename; Wine SIGUSR1
  handling; per-API apitrace wrapper name), each fixed with a regression test.
  The GDB harness and `core-grep` independently reproduced plant 10 slot value
  `0x006C342B` at `0x005B1AA4`, agreeing with the prior manual
  `RUN-GDB-20260812-153150`. The `rr` block is Wine's `ntsync` driver, not a
  wrapper defect and not a GPU/disk issue.
- **Consequences:** [[Runtime Tooling]], [[Known Unknowns]] (`KU-028`/`029`/
  `030`), [[Environment Status]], and [[Current State]] record the validated
  status and the two established defaults. The `rr` reader-detection avenue is
  blocked until `/dev/ntsync` is removed (`sudo modprobe -r ntsync`, or an
  unprivileged namespace that hides it); `perf_event_paranoid=1` is a
  runtime-only change that reverts on reboot.
- **Revisit when:** `/dev/ntsync` is removed and `rr` record→replay is retried;
  the frozen-frame perturbation question (`KU-029`) or the apitrace EGL-stream
  discrimination (`KU-030`, `KU-012`) is taken up; or a capture backend other
  than gamescope is needed.

## DEC-20260812-021 - End field widening at the selected-entry event boundary

- **Context:** `EXP-20260812-027` finds no read of either exact plant 10 entry
  `+0x28` word through guarded batch return or an unattended title. Together,
  M2.9–M2.14 now give every exact entry field populated by `0x00448010` and
  its guarded caller at least one explicit read-watchpoint window. Static
  instructions `0x0044CE0D–0x0044CE3E` independently expose a per-resource
  header-to-entry loop with an exact `+0x2E` read.
- **Decision:** Stop field-by-field widening. Make M2.15 a controlled
  post-title event trace of exact plant 10 `+0x2E` words and test whether
  `0x0044CE3E` is the first reader. Require runtime selector 10 at
  `[edi-0x08]`, the measured slot/header, exact entry address/value, branch,
  and first callee before promoting the candidate into the plant 10 chain.
- **Alternatives:** Watch unpopulated entry offsets; repeat an unbounded idle
  gameplay window; infer the entries are unused; begin semantic `.ALL`
  parsing; promote `0x0044CA00` from static structure alone.
- **Reasoning:** The exact candidate turns the next packet into an event and
  ownership discriminator instead of another arbitrary memory-range search.
  It preserves the negative-window limits and creates a direct route toward a
  live consumer without assigning field or function semantics.
- **Consequences:** M2.14 closes as a bounded negative. `0x0044CA00` receives
  an address-based dossier with unknown status. M2.15 begins at the natural
  title and uses a bounded first transition/`NextLife` endpoint; no resource
  editor, placement identity, or cross-port claim is promoted.
- **Revisit when:** `0x0044CE3E` reads either exact plant 10 word, another
  first reader wins, or the bounded post-title event completes with no read.

## DEC-20260812-022 - Maintain a capability-gap register without competing with the roadmap

- **Context:** The runtime-tooling uplift proved several tools and also exposed
  recurring friction: fixed one-phase GDB specs, a four-slot hardware limit,
  ad hoc remote input, manual renderer proof, an `ntsync` block on replay, and
  missing reference environments. The researcher requested a durable Obsidian
  view of what would help continued reverse engineering and a reusable
  template.
- **Decision:** Maintain [[Reverse Engineering Capability Gaps]] as a
  descriptive register of observed limitation, evidence, workaround, desired
  acceptance test, priority, and authority boundary. Use [[Capability Gap]]
  when an item needs its own dossier. Keep [[Project Roadmap]] authoritative
  for work ordering, [[Environment Status]] authoritative for installed state,
  and [[Known Unknowns]] authoritative for factual uncertainty. Recording a
  gap does not authorize sudo, root changes, media acquisition, persistent
  configuration, broader screen capture, or target mutation.
- **Alternatives:** Put feature wishes in session notes; turn the register into
  a second backlog; treat every missing convenience as a blocker; leave tool
  needs implicit in experiments.
- **Reasoning:** A linked evidence-oriented register tells the researcher what
  would materially improve the work without losing provenance or silently
  expanding authority. Acceptance tests keep entries actionable and allow
  resolved features to move to their authoritative environment/tool notes.
- **Consequences:** CAP-001 through CAP-009 now expose immediate, medium, and
  deferred needs. The top current needs are staged conditional debugger
  capture, deterministic receipt-bearing PC input, and an `rr`-compatible Wine
  environment. The register is linked from the implementation and reverse-
  engineering MOCs and [[Runtime Tooling]].
- **Revisit when:** A roadmap dependency changes, a tool is live-validated, a
  workaround fails, or the register starts duplicating scheduling or factual
  state rather than pointing to the authoritative note.

## DEC-20260812-023 - Quarantine the M2.15 debugger incident and require a fresh simultaneous chain

- **Context:** Accepted M2.15 arms record zero plant 10 `+0x2E` accesses from
  the natural title through `NextLife`, zero accesses in a separate
  30.026628-second post-title interval, and no execution of exact reader
  `0x0044CE3E` in a separate 10.014330-second control. A later exploratory
  caller-gate arm recorded one stop at `0x0044D79A`, followed in that run's
  raw GDB output by `SIGSEGV` at the same PC. The harness nevertheless reported
  cleanup and detach, and Wine later printed the matching unhandled read fault.
  The exact instruction has no memory operand.
- **Decision:** Complete M2.15 from the three pre-incident accepted arms only.
  Retain the two later run directories and contradiction, but do not use them
  to promote the outer gate, call, callee, or zero-stop interval. Make M2.16 a
  fresh-process arm with gate `0x0044D79A`, callee `0x0044CA00`, and both exact
  plant 10 data watches installed simultaneously. Require an explicit live-game
  endpoint after cleanup/detach.
- **Alternatives:** Hide the fault and accept the gate chain; discard all
  M2.15 evidence; extend another idle watch; treat a harness cleanup receipt as
  sufficient game-survival proof; assign semantics from static branches.
- **Reasoning:** The accepted no-access windows ended before the contradictory
  instrumentation and remain independently bounded. The later stop is useful
  incident evidence but cannot prove a continuation. A fresh simultaneous arm
  avoids missing a one-shot gate between sequential runs and directly tests
  whether CAP-001 must block further use.
- **Consequences:** M2.15 closes without a consumer promotion. `KU-031` records
  the unresolved tooling cause. M2.16 rejects any arm with a Wine exception or
  missing survival receipt; recurrence moves staged debugger hardening ahead of
  further caller-chain interpretation.
- **Revisit when:** The fresh four-point arm completes with normal survival,
  the same fault reproduces, or a harness fixture/live test isolates the cause.

## DEC-20260812-024 - Treat M2.16 as a reproduced tooling fault and harden signal handling first

- **Context:** `EXP-20260812-029` executes DEC-023's discriminator in a fresh
  unchanged process with outer gate `0x0044D79A`, callee `0x0044CA00`, and
  both exact plant 10 watches armed simultaneously. It records one outer-gate
  stop. Raw GDB immediately records `SIGSEGV` at the same PC, while schema 1
  continues the unclassified non-`SIGINT` signal and later reports
  `target_reached`, cleanup, and detach because `NextLife` appeared in the
  log. Wine opens winedbg, and the isolated post-detach game frame is a fatal-
  error dialog rather than live gameplay.
- **Decision:** Close M2.16 under its explicit fault-reproduction done
  condition and accept the run only as KU-031/CAP-001 tooling evidence. Do not
  promote the static gate-to-callee edge or interpret the absence of callee/
  watch stops. Make M2.17 an unexpected-fatal-signal-safe harness packet:
  synthetic fixture first, structured terminal signal/PC, demonstrated safe
  cleanup/detach, independent configurable survival receipt, then a previously
  safe live watchpoint. Do not return to `0x0044D79A` until those gates pass.
- **Alternatives:** Treat `NextLife` or harness cleanup/detach as sufficient;
  accept the post-fault zero stops as a bounded negative; retry the same live
  arm; infer normal completion from static branch semantics; abandon the
  debugger harness entirely.
- **Reasoning:** Raw signal output and the crash dialog directly measure target
  survival and contradict the structured status. Code inspection explains why
  schema 1 omits the fatal stop but not why the initial fault occurs. A fixture
  can safely discriminate signal-control behavior before risking another live
  caller trace, while a safe live control checks Wine-specific regressions.
- **Consequences:** CAP-001 is blocking rather than merely high priority.
  `NextLife` remains a valid log milestone but is never a standalone survival
  receipt. M2 consumer interpretation pauses; field/function names stay
  address-based. Preferred future launches use the independently validated
  windowed/muted launcher options without changing the evidence gates.
- **Revisit when:** the fatal-signal fixture, independent survival receipt, and
  safe live control pass; then design a new bounded discriminator for the gate
  without assuming this hardware-breakpoint continuation is safe.

## DEC-20260812-025 - Fail closed on target signals and require an independent survival receipt

- **Context:** Schema-1 GDB control could continue an unexpected `SIGSEGV`,
  allow an appended log milestone to win, and report `target_reached` after the
  game had entered Wine's fatal path. M2.17 needed a safe fix without breaking
  old watch specs or Wine's normal thread signaling.
- **Decision:** Adopt `gdb-harness` 1.1.0/result schema 2. Continue accepting
  strict watch-spec schema 1; add schema 2's bounded survival block. Configure
  `SIGSEGV/SIGILL/SIGBUS/SIGFPE/SIGABRT/SIGSYS` as terminal
  `stop print nopass`, treat every observed non-allowlisted target signal as a
  structured terminal result with signal/PC, and keep only `SIGUSR1/SIGUSR2`
  as explicit routine pass-through. Gather the survival receipt only after
  cleanup/detach; a required failure forces `accepted == false` even when the
  primary window otherwise completed.
- **Alternatives:** Parse raw GDB text after each run; trust cleanup/detach or
  `NextLife`; deliver the pending fatal signal on detach; disable all target
  signals; make schema 2 mandatory for existing specs; retry `0x0044D79A`
  before validating a safe live control.
- **Reasoning:** The fixture safely proves that `nopass`, point deletion, and
  detach preserve the deliberately injected signal target, while independent
  `/proc` identity/state/tracer samples separate target survival from the
  debugger controller's own cleanup. Signal inspection before milestone
  acceptance directly closes the reproduced false-success race.
- **Consequences:** `EXP-20260812-030` completes M2.17: synthetic fatal,
  required-failure, routine-signal, and prior safe live-watch gates pass.
  CAP-001's fatal/survival subset moves to resolved; staged phases, predicates,
  and register-relative reads remain open. KU-031 narrows to why continuation
  at exact `0x0044D79A` faults. M2.18 may compare an adjacent same-path PC with
  that exact PC because either signal now terminates and rejects its arm.
- **Revisit when:** a Wine version requires another routine signal, `/proc`
  checks prove insufficient for a named endpoint, or matched adjacent/exact-PC
  runs localize the remaining continuation fault.

## DEC-20260812-026 - Leave the unsafe caller gate and return to M2's rendered outcome

- **Context:** M2.18 `RUN-GDB-20260812-221224` arms only adjacent same-path
  `0x0044D794` in a fresh unchanged Direct3D run. It stops once and then
  receives structured `SIGSEGV` at the same PC. Schema 2 correctly rejects
  the arm, deletes the point, detaches, and proves five resumed/untraced
  samples plus later live game-only Training under `nopass`. Independent fresh
  runs already reproduce the corresponding same-PC fault at exact
  `0x0044D79A`. M2's goal remains a level/resource-to-rendered dependency and
  one useful editable asset family; continued caller-gate/tooling work risks
  drifting from that exit gate.
- **Decision:** Complete M2.18 after the adjacent-failure stop condition and do
  not run its conditional fresh exact arm. Treat hardware execution
  breakpoints at `0x0044D794/0x0044D79A` as unsafe caller-chain evidence in
  this Wine/native-GDB environment. Make M2.19 a direct visible-outcome packet:
  reuse the already accepted exact plant-04-to-plant-10 pair replacement,
  apply one identical bounded Training camera trajectory in matched fresh
  hardware arms, repeat the unchanged control, and compare only isolated game
  frames against measured control variance.
- **Alternatives:** Keep probing adjacent instructions; implement staged or
  register-relative GDB features first; rerun exact `0x0044D79A` despite the
  packet stop rule; accept target survival as continuation proof; move to M3;
  declare M2 complete without a resource-linked rendered outcome.
- **Reasoning:** Two neighboring same-path execution-breakpoint failures make
  exact-address localization unproductive for the current milestone. The
  hardened controller has already served its safety purpose. Existing paired-
  replacement acceptance, exact path locality, loader anchors, windowed/muted
  hardware launch, scoped input, and game-only capture are sufficient for a
  direct attempt at M2's missing outcome without new infrastructure.
- **Consequences:** KU-031 remains open as a nearby-region tooling/environment
  issue outside M2.19's critical path. No callee/data semantics are promoted.
  M2.19 has a strict single-trajectory stop rule: a repeated positive can
  complete the rendered bridge; a repeated bounded negative retires this
  plant-pair route rather than expanding the same session.
- **Revisit when:** a different roadmap packet has a concrete need for this
  caller gate and demonstrates a safer continuation method, or M2.19's direct
  visible-outcome result selects a different resource family.

## DEC-20260812-027 - Retire the plant visible route and select a one-path character discriminator

- **Context:** M2.19 `EXP-20260812-032` runs fresh unchanged/replacement/
  unchanged hardware arms along one fixed Training trajectory. The two
  controls are byte-identical at 11 of 12 same-index samples. Replacement
  residuals follow animation and camera-transition edges; no solid stable
  replacement-local region survives direct, temporal-median, or three-sample
  mask comparisons. The packet's stop rule requires retiring the plant-04/10
  route after this repeated negative. The PC census independently records a
  narrower candidate: Training loads `CHARS/FLICK.ANM/.ALL`; the
  `CHARS3/FLICKD.ANM` counterpart is byte-identical, while equal-size
  `FLICK.ALL`/`FLICKD.ALL` differ.

- **Context:** M2.31 specified a 23-location non-source influence union, exact

  payload-free receipt at existing safe pre-call/entry-post data watches.
  M2.29's accepted three arms independently share one 168-call plan and fixed
  form/family/root/page/survival envelope, but retained no predicate state.
- **Decision:** Complete only M2.32's offline gate in `EXP-20260813-045`.
  Adopt harness 1.5.0/schema 6 `exact_0a6c_admission` as a purpose-specific,
  fail-closed extension that locks the executable, existing three data
  watches, accepted forms/families, entry 7/offset `0x0A6C`/page 16, required
  survival, and canonical 168-call plan SHA-256
  `5eb280a7bd54b56a9654fed7984e9677a15618c5376be703a85718ea3bb2ffec`.
  Retain only first reject/evaluated count/active-state digest/predicted and
  observed append agreement plus zero-payload declarations; forcibly redact
  raw stop values. Keep live matched arms behind fresh explicit authority.
- **Alternatives:** Build a general predicate/expression engine; add inner
  execution breakpoints or another watch; serialize the source, active state,
  projected values, or batch counts; relax the complete-plan digest; infer a
  result from M2.29's old arms; launch immediately; edit the source; widen to
  another record/family; create general `.ALL` tooling; or consult PS1.
- **Reasoning:** Synthetic coverage reproduces every specified reject path,
  both depth branches and skip, native arithmetic failures, digest drift,
  count disagreement, exact controller closure, and output exclusion. All
  three accepted M2.29 specs independently agree on the locked plan and fixed
  safety envelope. Hard-coding this one evidence question prevents an offline
  convenience from becoming an unaudited general capture surface.
- **Consequences:** M2.32's offline implementation gate is complete and
  harness schemas 1–5 remain compatible. Schema 6 exact mode is fixture-
  validated, not live-validated. KU-044 now carries only live agreement,
  temporal first-reject stability, and the existing source/visible-causality
  limits. No runtime, target write, source edit, payload retention, semantic
  naming, parser/editor, public mod operation, M1 identity bridge, or
  cross-port claim is promoted.
- **Revisit when:** The user freshly authorizes the exact matched live phase,
  a fixture or review exposes an implementation mismatch, or a future packet
  has independent evidence and authority to supersede this one-record schema.

## DEC-20260813-041 - Treat exact admission as temporal before another source attribution

- **Context:** Freshly authorized `EXP-20260813-046` live-validates harness
  1.5.0/schema 6 at exact entry 7/offset `0x0A6C`. Prediction agrees with the
  native count outcome in all three accepted arms, but the fresh pattern is
  `0/0/1`: control A and the replacement reject at `0x004065B0`, while
  unchanged control B passes all 12 slots and appends. This does not reproduce
  M2.29's retained `0/1/0` and fails M2.32's stable-control/replacement-pass
  selection gate.
- **Decision:** Complete M2.32 under its explicit temporal-ambiguity outcome.
  Do not select a non-source input or attribute admission to the replacement.
  Make M2.33 an offline-first audit for a fixed consecutive-cycle receipt in
  one unchanged post-`NextLife` process, reusing only the existing evaluator,
  three data watches, complete plan, count reconciliation, and payload-free
  row. Keep any repeated-cycle runtime behind new explicit authority.
- **Alternatives:** Normalize the new result to M2.29; choose control A's
  slot-7 rejection as replacement evidence; discard control B; retain raw
  active-state values; add inner code points or watches; edit `FLICK.ALL` now;
  widen to another record/family; build a general predicate or `.ALL` tool;
  assign orientation/primitive/pixel semantics; or consult PS1.
- **Reasoning:** Independent prediction/count agreement proves the evaluator
  is not the source of the contradiction. The same unchanged file both
  rejects and passes across fresh processes, while the replacement does not
  uniquely pass. One-cycle-per-process receipts cannot distinguish variation
  within a process from launch-specific state, so a bounded ordered temporal
  sequence is the smallest discriminating next question.
- **Consequences:** Schema 6 exact mode is now fixture- and live-validated at
  its locked one-record envelope. KU-044 resolves at evaluator agreement and
  rejected stable attribution; KU-045 carries only consecutive-cycle
  stability. No active-state value, source/visible causality, semantic name,
  parser/editor, public mod operation, other record/family, target/source
  write, execution point, watch slot, or cross-port claim is promoted.
- **Revisit when:** M2.33's offline audit proves a safe fixed-cycle envelope,
  exposes a controller/ceiling blocker, or separately authorized live evidence
  bounds within-process temporal behavior.

## DEC-20260813-042 - Fix the exact temporal receipt at three captured cycles

- **Context:** M2.32 live-validates one exact payload-free receipt per fresh
  process but unchanged controls disagree. M2.33 must distinguish a stable
  within-process result from temporal recurrence without adding watches, raw
  values, or a general capture surface. The accepted exact arms each consumed
  exactly 400 stops for one warmup plus one captured cycle.
- **Decision:** Complete M2.33's offline gate with harness 1.6.0/schema 6 and
  exactly three captured cycles after the existing one warmup. Preserve
  legacy `capture_cycles=1`; accept `capture_cycles=3` only for the temporal
  envelope and reject other exact counts. Reuse the canonical 168-call plan,
  existing three data watches, exact receipt, 1,000-stop/90-second ceilings,
  redaction, and all survival/identity gates. Keep live use behind fresh
  explicit authority.
- **Alternatives:** Use two captured cycles; consume four captured cycles at
  the exact stop ceiling; allow arbitrary counts through the generic batch
  limit; add a summary or raw-state row; add watches or execution points;
  edit the source; widen to another record/family; build general `.ALL`
  tooling; assign semantics; launch immediately; or consult PS1.
- **Reasoning:** Two cycles can show one transition but not recurrence; three
  can fixture stable reject, stable pass, and `A/B/A`. The three independent
  accepted exact arms establish a 200-stop complete-cycle rhythm, so one
  warmup plus three captures projects to 800 stops and retains 200 stops of
  margin. Existing controller closure emits only complete plan/candidate
  cycles and naturally excludes a partial final cycle from result output.
- **Consequences:** `EXP-20260813-047` fixture-proves the fixed envelope and
  payload exclusion without changing schema 6 or receipt fields. KU-045
  narrows to live temporal behavior. No runtime evidence, source/visible
  causality, semantic name, parser/editor, public mod operation, other
  record/family, target/source write, new point/slot, or cross-port claim is
  promoted.
- **Revisit when:** The user freshly authorizes one unchanged post-`NextLife`
  three-cycle arm, the observed stop rhythm violates the retained ceiling, or
  a fixture/review exposes a closure or redaction defect.

## DEC-20260813-043 - Close temporal admission at bounded recurrence and audit M2

- **Context:** Freshly authorized `EXP-20260813-048` runs the fixed M2.33
  envelope in one unchanged post-`NextLife` process. All three predictions
  agree with independent count outcomes and produce ordered
  reject/pass/reject (`0/1/0`). Both rejects occur at `0x004065B0` after seven
  slots, but all three active-state digests differ. The arm completes in 816
  stops, 184 below the unchanged ceiling, and passes every renderer,
  redaction, cleanup/detach, signal, and survival gate.
- **Decision:** Complete M2.33 at the demonstrated within-process `A/B/A`
  recurrence. Do not infer a period, distribution, equal underlying reject
  state, source causality, or visible causality, and do not deepen the same
  temporal capture. Make M2.34 an offline-only M2 exit-gate reconciliation:
  test the accumulated PC-native level/resource/outcome and replacement-
  instruction evidence against the four written milestone gates, closing M2
  if they are satisfied or naming exactly one remaining blocker if not.
- **Alternatives:** Treat one sequence as periodic; retain raw state to explain
  the differing digests; run more cycles or processes immediately; attribute
  the middle append to source bytes; begin a source edit/parser; move to M3
  without checking M2's written gates.
- **Reasoning:** The fixed discriminator has answered its bounded question:
  admission variation occurs within one unchanged process and can recur over
  three captures. More observations would estimate behavior rather than close
  the existing causal gap. An explicit gate audit is now the smallest way to
  prevent a 33-packet milestone from continuing by inertia or closing with an
  unstated level/resource bridge assumption.
- **Consequences:** KU-045 resolves only at bounded within-process recurrence.
  Harness 1.6.0/schema 6 is live-validated at both accepted exact counts; it
  remains a hard-coded evidence tool, not a general temporal interface. No
  source/target write, payload, semantic name, parser/editor, public mod
  operation, other record/family, or cross-port claim is promoted. M2.34 adds
  no live or implementation authority.
- **Revisit when:** the M2.34 audit shows that all four gates are met, exposes
  one precise unsupported bridge or distribution requirement, or discovers a
  contradiction in the retained evidence.

## DEC-20260813-044 - Keep M2 open on one record-to-resource selection edge

- **Context:** `EXP-20260813-049` reconciles the accumulated M2 evidence
  against the four written exit gates. `EXP-20260812-017` supplies exact
  `.ANM` first-level coverage across 11 PC files plus 16 synthetic tests and
  preserves unknown bytes without a writer. `EXP-20260812-033` supplies an
  accepted, exact-hash `FLICK.ALL <- FLICKD.ALL` whole-file recipe whose paths,
  hashes, staging checks, and instructions can be distributed without either

  static/runtime/visible anchors, but no cited PC edge makes one select the
  other. M2.23's formerly labeled hardware arena cannot supply that edge
  because `EXP-20260813-039` proves it is selector-1 software output.
- **Decision:** Complete M2.34 with three supported gates and keep M2 active on
  exactly one unsupported dependency-path gate: the missing M1 scene-record/
  positive-target-to-ID-zero-resource selection edge in `KU-023`. Treat the
  selected `.ANM` format gate as satisfied only at its documented first-level
  boundary; do not transfer it to `.ALL`. Treat the exact M2.20 copy as an
  asset-free instruction recipe, not a generic `.ALL` editor or compatibility
  rule. Make M2.35 an offline selector-2 provenance slice inside exact M1
  consumer `0x0045A7F0`.
- **Alternatives:** Close M2 by treating shared renderer output as resource
  identity; fail the `.ANM` format gate because the visible discriminator used
  `.ALL`; claim `.ALL` validation from two exact files; require a public
  manifest operation even though the gate permits instructions; continue
  temporal capture or inspect another family before reconciling the gates.
- **Reasoning:** The gate wording permits the explicitly selected, tested
  `.ANM` format boundary and an independently exact replacement instruction,
  while the milestone dependency path still requires a real selection edge.
  Preserving the format mismatch and corrected renderer ownership avoids two
  silent promotions. The exact selector-2 arguments in `0x0045A7F0` are the
  smallest remaining PC-native surface that could carry the missing edge.
- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]] and [[Current State]] advance to
  offline M2.35. `KU-023` becomes the sole M2 exit blocker. No runtime, target
  or source edit, parser/editor, manifest/runtime schema, `.ALL` semantic,
  resource identity, other family, or cross-port claim is authorized or
  promoted.
- **Revisit when:** M2.35 proves an exact ID-zero/resource-selection edge,
  classifies every relevant selector-2 input with a complete negative, or
  exposes a contradiction requiring the M2 gate itself to be reconsidered.

## DEC-20260813-045 - Close the positive-target selector-2 bridge and rank generic indices

- **Context:** `EXP-20260813-050` reviews both selector-2 branches of exact M1

- **Decision:** Close M2.35 as a complete negative at the `0x0045A7F0`
  selector-2 boundary. Do not reopen it from batch-root convergence, draw
  calls, or overlapping pixels. Make M2.36 an offline producer census limited
  to the eight generic indexed readers from `EXP-20260813-035`, testing whether
  an index on the normal M1 coordinator/selected-record route derives from an
  M1 record or payload and proves resource ID zero.
- **Alternatives:** Treat the shared Direct3D roots as identity; interpret
  nearby scratch `0x005B1ED0` as header `0x005B18E0`; launch another runtime

  carries index zero.
- **Reasoning:** The exact renderer candidate is now exhausted with both
  structured and native evidence. The previously enumerated eight-reader set

- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]], [[Current State]], and `KU-023`

## DEC-20260813-047 - Retain the M2 gate on one finite signed-selector candidate

- **Context:** `EXP-20260813-052` reconciles the literal M2 outcome and first

  alternative: at most 20 signed selector words are used in
  `0x005B1A7C + selector * 4`, and selector `-103` addresses exact ID-zero
  header `0x005B18E0`.
- **Decision:** Complete M2.37 with option (b). Keep the dependency-path gate
  and M2 active. Select only the 20-word `0x0059D9C0 + i * 0x30`,
  `0 <= i < 20`, signed-selector surface in `0x0044CA00` for offline M2.38.
  Test whether the established M1 record/positive-target route produces one
  of those words and whether it can be signed `-103` at `0x0044CE0D`.
- **Alternatives:** Close M2 by treating the two parallel visible chains as
  one dependency; weaken the gate to any level-resident load request; infer a
  selection edge from shared Direct3D output; widen to persistent globals,
  indirect calls, more xrefs, or another family without ranking; resume the
  quarantined execution-point route.
- **Reasoning:** The retained gate still describes a practical causal outcome,
  while the selected candidate is finite, PC-native, in the existing M1
  coordinator, post-record in call order, and capable of addressing ID zero
  by exact signed arithmetic. That ranking does not assert that the source
  array is M1-derived or ever contains `-103`.
- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]], [[Current State]], and `KU-023`
  advance to offline M2.38. M2 remains active. No runtime, mutation,
  parser/schema, semantic name, new xref/index family, other resource family,
  or cross-port work is authorized or promoted.
- **Revisit when:** M2.38 proves an exact M1-derived signed `-103` selector
  lineage to `0x005B18E0` or completes the bounded 20-word producer/value
  negative and retires this candidate.

## DEC-20260813-048 - Retire the signed-selector candidate without implicit widening

- **Context:** `EXP-20260813-053` classifies every native writer for selector

- **Decision:** Complete M2.38 with a bounded current-invocation negative and

  open. Do not infer an edge from persistent-state possibility, and do not
  select another index/resource family or whole-program indirect search as an
  automatic successor. Further substantive work requires a separate recorded
  scope disposition.

  populated the array; reinterpret an in-table positive selector as `-103`;
  promote arbitrary indirect aliasing or malformed input into a native
  producer; widen immediately to another family; or close M2 despite the
  unsatisfied causal gate.
- **Reasoning:** Exact native stores, source storage, table bytes, and call
  order all agree on the negative. The packet's explicit boundary forbids
  converting unproved persistent or indirect possibilities into evidence or
  an unranked next search.
- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]], [[Current State]], `KU-023`, and the
  relevant dossiers record M2.38 complete and negative. M2 remains active but
  has no implicitly authorized successor candidate. No runtime, mutation,
  parser/schema, semantic rename, new index/resource family, broad indirect
  search, or cross-port work is authorized by this decision.
- **Revisit when:** A separate scope decision selects one bounded PC-native
  discriminator, revises the milestone plan without weakening its causal
  outcome, or external evidence exposes a contradiction in the producer
  census.

## DEC-20260813-049 - Defer M2 and advance the proven whole-file mod boundary

- **Status:** Superseded in part by `DEC-20260813-050`. The retained causal
  gate and M4.1 technical boundary stand; M2 deferral and exclusive M4.1
  activation do not.
- **Context:** `EXP-20260813-053` completes M2.38 with a finite negative and
  retires the only candidate selected by the prior scope disposition. M2's
  first exit gate remains unsupported under `KU-023`, but the completed
  direct-reader, selector-2 input, generic-index, and signed-selector searches
  leave no ranked finite successor. Separately, M1 tooling is promoted and
  `EXP-20260812-033/049` support one exact, accepted, asset-free whole-file
  replacement recipe. The roadmap permits M4 capabilities to accumulate from
  proven earlier work, while M3's entry condition still requires the missing
  bridge.
- **Decision:** Complete M2.39 as an offline scope disposition. Keep M2's
  causal gate and `KU-023` unchanged, move M2 from `Active` to `Deferred`, and
  do not open an unranked index/resource-family or whole-program indirect
  search. Keep M3 planned. Make M4 active on M4.1: a versioned, fail-closed,
  exact-hash whole-file replacement manifest boundary for user-owned work-copy
  files, integrated with the existing non-destructive staging and receipt
  workflow.
- **Alternatives:** Close M2 by merging the parallel rendered chains; keep M2
  active without an exact packet; label M2 blocked despite no external
  dependency; select another family or persistent-state sweep without ranking;
  activate M3 despite its unmet entry condition; or generalize the exact
  replacement into `.ALL` compatibility or format semantics.
- **Reasoning:** `Complete` would contradict the causal gate, `Active` would
  contradict the one-work-packet rule, and `Blocked` is reserved for a named
  external dependency. `Deferred` preserves the evidence boundary and exact
  revisit conditions. M4.1 converts a demonstrated resource change into
  reversible user value without inventing the missing M1-to-resource edge or
  distributing protected content.
- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]], [[Current State]], and `KU-023` record
  M2 as incomplete and deferred. M4 becomes the sole active milestone with one
  bounded packet. M4.1 may change manifest/tooling code and add synthetic/local
  hash checks, but it may not bundle game assets, parse or edit `.ALL`, claim
  general compatibility or identity, launch the game, open another family, or
  consult PS1.
- **Revisit when:** Cited PC evidence exposes a finite M2 dependency candidate,
  a concrete mod goal supplies a bounded discriminator for `KU-023`, a
  contradiction invalidates a completed negative, or M4.1 review shows that
  exact whole-file replacement cannot preserve the existing safety contract.

## DEC-20260813-050 - Reverse premature M2 deferral and rank fixed-pool indices

- **Context:** The adversarial `EXP-20260813-055` audit tested every premise
  behind `DEC-20260813-049`. M2's causal gate remains open, the M2.38 negative
  survives, and M4.1 remains technically evidence-backed. The priority premise
  fails: M2.36 had already preserved exact unresolved inputs
  `0x0059B730/0x0059B7B4` at one generic header reader. They are first dwords
  of slots 2 and 3 in the bounded 64-record `0x84`-stride pool, and each
  selects `0x005B18E0 + I * 4`; exact zero selects ID zero. M2.39 did not
  census their producers.
- **Decision:** Supersede the deferral and exclusive M4.1 activation in
  `DEC-20260813-049`. Restore M2 to `Active` and select offline M2.40: classify
  every native direct or bounded fixed-pool producer capable of reaching only
  `0x0059B730/0x0059B7B4`, decide reader-time zero reachability, and intersect
  its source lineage with the established M1 record/payload route. Return M4
  and M4.1 to `Planned`; preserve M4.1's exact-hash user-owned-file boundary.
- **Alternatives:** Defend the prior deferral because no successor had been
  formally ranked; discard M4.1 despite its surviving evidence; widen to all
  persistent globals, all 64 pool slots, another resource family, arbitrary
  indirect aliases, or runtime mutation; or call initial pool zero an M1-
  derived reader-time selection.
- **Reasoning:** "No ranked successor" describes a roadmap state, not the
  evidence universe. The two words are exact, finite, already cited, and use
  the selected ID-zero formula. Native pool initialization proves only initial
  zero, so a bounded producer/reachability census remains necessary. M4.1's
  technical feasibility supports retaining it as planned work but cannot
  establish that it should displace a viable causal-gate experiment.
- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]], [[Current State]], `KU-023`, and the
  relevant MOCs restore M2/M2.40 as the sole active milestone/packet. M4.1 is
  queued without schema or code changes. `EXP-20260813-054` is marked
  superseded rather than silently rewritten. M2.40 is offline only and does
  not authorize other fixed-pool objects, another family, runtime, mutation,
  parser/schema work, semantic renames, or PS1 evidence.
- **Revisit when:** M2.40 proves an exact M1-derived zero at either native read,
  completes a precise producer negative for both words, or finds an evidence
  contradiction requiring a separately bounded disposition.

## DEC-20260813-051 - Retire the audited fixed-pool candidate and resume M4.1

- **Status:** Superseded in part by `DEC-20260813-052`. M2.40's declared
  slot-2/slot-3 result stands; M2 deferral and exclusive M4.1 activation do
  not.

- **Context:** `EXP-20260813-055` correctly found that M2.39 had deferred M2
  before classifying exact fixed-pool inputs `0x0059B730/0x0059B7B4`.
  `EXP-20260813-056` now completes that missing census. The complete bounded
  first-dword store set is pool clear `0x0041C430`, retained-source copy
  `0x0041BD24`, and conditional removal zero `0x0041BBBA`. Initial clear zero
  cannot pass the reader gates. Conditional removal zero is statically
  reader-reachable, but its value and predicate sources are the separate
  `LEVEL.RAW` type-`0x23` table plus fixed-pool state. No M1 20-byte record,
  relocated payload, or positive target reaches this surface.
- **Decision:** Complete M2.40 with a bounded causal negative and retire the
  fixed-pool candidate. Keep the M2 causal gate and `KU-023` open, but return
  M2 to `Deferred` because no separately ranked finite successor remains; do
  not widen implicitly. Restore M4 as the sole active milestone on exact
  packet M4.1, preserving its fail-closed, exact-hash, asset-free boundary over
  user-owned work-copy files.
- **Alternatives:** Treat the initial pool clear as reader-time selection;
  treat a conditional separate-source zero as M1-derived; demand runtime
  observation even though every proven predicate source is outside M1; leave
  M2 active without a packet; widen to another persistent object/index family;
  close M2 despite the missing causal edge; or generalize M4.1 into `.ALL`
  parsing or compatibility.
- **Reasoning:** The hard-truth audit's concrete objection has now been
  executed rather than dismissed. Exact store, caller, gate, update-order, and
  source-lineage evidence agree: zero can reach the reader, but not from M1.
  This resolves the ranked candidate without satisfying the milestone. M4.1
  remains the smallest already evidenced packet that creates user value while
  preserving all prior safety boundaries.
- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]], [[Current State]], `KU-023`, relevant
  function dossiers, and MOCs record M2.40 complete, M2 deferred, and M4.1
  active. No runtime or game-data mutation was performed. No other pool,
  resource/index family, parser/schema surface, semantic field name, or PS1
  evidence is opened by this decision.
- **Revisit when:** A cited PC-native result exposes a finite M1-to-resource
  candidate, a concrete mod goal supplies a bounded discriminator for
  `KU-023`, or evidence contradicts the completed M2.40 writer/source census.

## DEC-20260813-052 - Reverse the second premature M2 deferral

- **Context:** `EXP-20260813-057` adversarially tests the post-M2.40
  disposition. M2.40 remains complete for fixed-pool slots 2/3, and its
  conditional zero remains separate-source rather than M1-derived. The
  priority premise fails twice. The first hard-truth report explicitly kept
  `0x0059D6A4` as lower-ranked slot 63, and M2.40 expressly excluded it.
  Separately, exact arithmetic supplies the pool classification omitted by the
  report's “separate object” label: `0x0059C30C` is slot 25:
  `0x0059B628 + 25 * 0x84 = 0x0059C30C`. Both first dwords feed exact generic
  `0x005B18E0 + I * 4` readers through fixed-pointer call sites.
- **Decision:** Supersede the M2 deferral and exclusive M4.1 activation in
  `DEC-20260813-051`. Restore M2 to `Active` on exact offline M2.41. Limit the
  packet to slot 25 at `0x0059C30C` through
  `0x004319CB -> 0x00459620` and slot 63 at `0x0059D6A4` through the five
  fixed-pointer calls to `0x004467E0/0x00446870`. Reuse M2.40's writer census,
  but decide the residual consumer gates, reader-time zero, and M1 lineage
  explicitly. Return M4/M4.1 to `Planned`; preserve M4.1's technical boundary.
- **Alternatives:** Defend deferral because the residual slots were not
  formally ranked; silently generalize M2.40 beyond its declared targets;
  treat same-pool membership as proof of the same reader-time result; widen to
  all 64 pool objects or all generic readers; close M2 despite its causal gate;
  or discard M4.1's still-valid exact-hash boundary.
- **Reasoning:** “No separately ranked successor” is a scheduling statement,
  not an evidence census. Slot 63 was explicitly preserved, while slot 25 was
  excluded without its exact pool classification. Both are exact, finite,
  PC-native, in the already reviewed pool/reader family, and can select ID zero
  by direct arithmetic. M2.40's common writer result is reusable evidence, but
  its slot-specific consumer gates and done condition cannot dispose other
  readers by implication.
- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]], [[Current State]], `KU-023`, and the
  relevant MOCs restore M2/M2.41 as the sole active milestone/packet. M4.1 is
  queued without implementation changes. No runtime, mutation, parser/schema,
  semantic rename, other pool/list object, new reader/resource family, broad
  indirect search, or PS1 evidence is authorized.
- **Revisit when:** M2.41 proves an exact M1-derived ID-zero selection through
  either residual slot, completes a precise reader/caller/source negative for
  both, or finds a contradiction requiring a separately bounded disposition.

## DEC-20260813-053 - Require an independent hard-truth ratchet for M2 disposition

- **Context:** `EXP-20260813-058` completes M2.41. Slot 25 zero can reach the
  ID-zero header and take `0x00459620`'s zero-return arm; slot 63 zero is
  excluded at two `I == 0x23` calls but can reach three state-gated ID-zero
  reads. The values and direct gates remain outside the established M1 route.
  Two earlier worker-authored M2 deferrals were nevertheless rejected because
  exact finite surfaces had been omitted or misclassified. The user requires a
  serial ratchet with an independent verifier before another disposition.
- **Decision:** Complete M2.41 with its bounded causal negative, but do not let
  the same worker defer or close M2. Keep M2 `Active` on exact offline M2.42.
  A fresh-context verifier must reread [[START HERE]] and the complete required
  Obsidian startup sequence, attempt to falsify the packet and milestone
  premises, and either accept the evidence-backed M2 disposition or reject it
  with one exact bounded PC-native successor. After a rejection, the next
  worker must repeat the startup refresh before executing that successor.
- **Alternatives:** Accept a third worker-authored deferral immediately; keep
  M2 active without an exact packet; ask the verifier for an unbounded search;
  treat static reachability as a named-scene observation; close M2 despite
  `KU-023`; or start M4.1 before the independent verdict.
- **Reasoning:** The ratchet preserves one canonical packet, makes rejection
  actionable, and separates evidence production from disposition authority.
  It also fails closed: a verifier cannot reject merely by asking for broader
  work, and a worker cannot convert "no candidate ranked" into "no candidate
  exists." M2.41's result remains evidence whether the verifier accepts or
  rejects its milestone conclusion.
- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]], [[Current State]], `KU-023`, and the
  relevant MOCs record M2.41 complete and M2.42 active. M4.1 stays planned.
  The verifier round must end with required Vault updates, an intentional Git
  commit, and a clean tree. No runtime, mutation, parser/schema, semantic
  rename, broad pool/resource-family search, or PS1 evidence is authorized.
- **Revisit when:** The independent verifier accepts an M2 disposition or
  rejects it with an exact successor. On rejection, reread the startup sequence
  and continue only that successor; on acceptance, apply the verified
  milestone status without inventing another packet.

## DEC-20260813-054 - Accept the M2.41 disposition and defer M2 without closure

- **Context:** Independent `EXP-20260813-059` executes the M2.42 serial

- **Alternatives:** Reject without an exact bounded successor; request a broad

- **Reasoning:** The two earlier omitted fixed-pool surfaces now have complete

  absence and cannot satisfy M2's causal gate.
- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]], [[Current State]], `KU-023`, relevant

  change, broad search, or cross-port evidence was used. M4.1 retains its

  claim general compatibility.
- **Revisit when:** Cited PC-native evidence exposes one exact finite
  M1-to-resource candidate, a concrete mod goal supplies a bounded

## DEC-20260814-055 - Extend the independent hard-truth ratchet through M4

- **Context:** The independent M2 ratchet prevented two premature deferrals and
  then accepted the third only after a fresh-context falsification pass. The
  user requires the same serial discipline for M4 going forward: one bounded
  worker packet, a clean intentional commit, and an independent verifier named
  James before another technical packet begins.
- **Decision:** Apply the ratchet to every M4 packet. Each worker begins by
  rereading [[START HERE]] and the complete required Obsidian startup sequence,
  executes only the exact active packet, updates the durable Vault records,
  commits intentionally, and leaves a clean tree. A fresh-context James round
  then attempts to falsify the result. James may accept only by naming one
  exact next M4 packet grounded in unmet exit gates, or reject only with one
  exact bounded corrective packet containing locators, question, first pass,
  stop rule, and done condition. After either verdict selects more work, the
  next worker repeats the startup refresh. Continue until James accepts M4
  completion or a genuine fail-closed blocker is recorded.
- **Alternatives:** Let the worker self-accept and choose the next packet; run
  worker and verifier in parallel against a changing tree; permit a vague
  broadening request after rejection; or postpone commits until several M4
  capabilities accumulate.
- **Reasoning:** Serial clean commits give the verifier one stable object to
  audit, while exact rejection requirements prevent both premature closure and
  unbounded review churn. Repeating startup after each verdict preserves the
  Obsidian evidence boundary across a potentially long automated sequence.
- **Consequences:** [[EXP-20260814-060 - Exact whole-file replacement manifest boundary]] records M4.1 as worker-complete, not independently accepted. Exact
  offline M4.2 is the sole next packet. No composition, dry-run, launcher,
  removal, compatibility, asset semantics, game launch, or M2 reopening is
  authorized until a verifier selects a bounded successor.
- **Revisit when:** James accepts M4 completion, three consecutive ratchet
  rounds reproduce the same genuine blocker with no safe bounded progress, or
  the user explicitly changes the verifier/serial-commit policy.

## DEC-20260814-056 - Reject M4.1 acceptance and correct the complete output plan

- **Context:** Independent `EXP-20260814-061` audits immutable clean commit
  `7879db6`. Forty focused and 228 full tests pass; all three M1 operations
  work under schema 2; and the exact ignored local recipe, deterministic
  receipt fields, source hashes, source preservation, and no-game-bytes commit
