  through an existing output symlink and returns success. A later
  non-directory destination parent leaves an earlier sparse write, while a
  source `.ablpc` file leaves a copied and modified staged tree before receipt
  failure. `--force` also accepts a directory destination and fails after an
  earlier write.
- **Decision:** Return `REJECT` for M4.2. Preserve the independently supported
  schema/hash/exact-recipe claims, but do not accept M4.1's complete-preflight
  or output-containment claims. Keep M4 active on exactly M4.3: preflight every
  planned manifest destination plus the fixed receipt path, close the four
  synthetic reproductions before any output mutation, and retain schema-1/
  schema-2 M1 plus the exact local recipe. Continue the serial James ratchet
  after the corrective worker commit.
- **Alternatives:** Accept because the declared suite is green; classify the
  outside receipt as an operator error; broaden immediately to transactional
  rollback for arbitrary I/O failures; or start composition/dry-run/launcher
  work while the non-destructive staging contract is false.
- **Reasoning:** The outside-root write directly violates containment, and the
  deterministic parent/type blockers directly violate complete preflight. All
  four share exact code boundaries in `_validate_output_destinations`,
  `_write_prepared`, and `_atomic_write`, so one finite corrective packet can
  close them without changing schema or adding a capability. Passing ordinary
  fixtures and the exact corpus recipe cannot override adversarial evidence.
- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]], [[Current State]], `KU-046`,
  [[M4 Exact Whole-file Replacement]], [[M1 Tool Support Matrix]], and the
  implementation MOC record M4.2 `REJECT` and M4.3 active. No implementation,
  game asset, original, runtime state, `.ALL` semantic, compatibility claim,
  M2 conclusion, or cross-port evidence changes in this audit.
- **Revisit when:** M4.3 closes all four reproductions with `ManifestError`
  before mutation, preserves the complete existing validation/local recipe,
  commits cleanly, and a fresh James round independently accepts or rejects
  that correction.

## DEC-20260814-064 - Reject M4.9 and require mounted-plan-path preflight

  bind-mounted intermediate overlay directory presents the exact authenticated
  visible tree; removal deletes the output through that mount and the receipt,
  then fails `EBUSY`, changing both overlay and independent target snapshots.

  M4.11. The correction is limited to rejecting any mountpoint or mount alias
  in the authenticated sparse-overlay deletion plan before the first unlink,
  with exact overlay/source/independent-target snapshots and every retained
  ordinary/local baseline.
- **Alternatives:** Reclassify a pre-existing mount as unpredictable I/O;
  accept partial deletion because the source tree survives; unmount paths on
  behalf of the user; add rollback/transactions; or abandon the already green
  ordinary sparse-overlay removal boundary.
- **Reasoning:** The mounted topology exists before preflight and is
  reproducible without a race. Current `lstat`/`scandir` type and tree checks
  authenticate visible content but not filesystem topology ownership. A
  bounded fail-closed topology guard addresses the falsification without
  changing schemas, receipts, operations, or the accepted M4.3/M4.7 contracts.
- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]], [[Current State]], `KU-048`,
  implementation notes/MOCs, README, and the session index record M4.10

  green, then a fresh-context verifier independently audits that commit.

## DEC-20260814-065 - Complete M4.11 and require independent M4.12

- **Context:** `EXP-20260814-069` rejected M4.9 because same-filesystem bind
  mounts are not distinguishable from ordinary directories by the existing
  `lstat`/`scandir` preflight. `EXP-20260814-070` adds one removal-only Linux
  mount-topology guard. It reads `/proc/self/mountinfo` before overlay
  traversal, decodes escaped byte paths, and checks the overlay root, every
  necessary directory, every recorded output, and the receipt against the
  mountpoint set. Private namespaces prove root/intermediate/output/receipt
  bind refusal 4/4 with complete snapshots unchanged. Focused/full suites pass
  99/287 and all six tracked local cycles retain exact hashes.
- **Decision:** Mark M4.11 worker-complete, not independently accepted. Keep M4
  active on exact fresh-context M4.12. James must independently falsify mount
  plan coverage, mountinfo parsing/failure, pre-unlink snapshot preservation,
  and ordinary unmounted behavior at the immutable clean worker commit before
  accepting the correction or selecting another M4 exit-gate packet.
- **Alternatives:** Treat `st_dev` or `lstat` as sufficient for bind mounts;
  unmount user paths; add rollback/general transactions; accept the worker
  without independent review; or begin a later toolkit capability before the
  rejected removal claim is closed.
- **Reasoning:** Linux mountinfo names bind mountpoints even when device and
  visible file types match the underlying filesystem, directly discriminating
  the stable topology that falsified M4.9. Fail-closed topology inspection is
  the smallest correction and does not expand deletion targets. The serial
  ratchet reserves acceptance for independent fresh-context review.
- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]], [[Current State]], `KU-048`,
  implementation notes/MOCs, README, and the session index record M4.11
  worker-complete and M4.12 active. Removal now requires trustworthy Linux
  `/proc/self/mountinfo`; no broader compatibility claim follows. No schema,
  receipt, operation, original, game asset, runtime, `.ALL`, M2, PS1,
  unmounting, staged/source deletion, or transaction behavior changes.

## DEC-20260814-066 - Reject M4.11 acceptance and cover proper overlay-root ancestry

- **Context:** Independent `EXP-20260814-071` audits immutable clean commit
  `b91712fb4cfa7412f867239292c0c3bc7e97828a`. The permitted non-mount focused/
  full suites pass 95/283 with the four namespace-backed cases deselected;
  removal, composition, and topology/schema/receipt selectors pass 32/17/28;
  12/12 pure-function mountinfo parser/failure checks pass; and all three
  tracked local families apply/remove twice with their exact hashes and
  unchanged sources. Acceptance nevertheless fails. A disposable
  James case and Root's independent repeat place an exact overlay below a
  pre-existing proper-parent alias to an independent parent. The exact overlay
  root and descendants are not mountpoints, cleanup returns success, and the
  independent overlay is removed. Both runs preserve source snapshots and
  change visible-overlay and independent-target snapshots. Static review shows
  `_validate_unmounted_deletion_plan` checks only the resolved overlay root and
  expected descendants, omitting the proper ancestry traversed to reach it.
- **Decision:** Return M4.12 `REJECT`, do not independently accept M4.11, and
  activate exactly M4.13. Extend only removal's mount-topology preflight input
  to include the relevant proper path ancestry traversed to reach the supplied/
  resolved overlay root. The correction must reject the proper-parent alias
  before traversal or unlink while keeping ordinary cleanup beneath normal
  filesystem ancestry usable and retaining every accepted baseline.
- **Alternatives:** Accept because the four exact planned-path mount cases
  pass; classify proper-parent traversal as unrelated to the deletion plan;
  reject every mountpoint ancestor including ordinary filesystem/root mounts;
  introduce a general mount-ownership policy; unmount user paths; or broaden
  into rollback/transactions.
- **Reasoning:** Every overlay read and deletion traverses the omitted proper
  parent, so its stable alias topology is part of safe reachability even though
  it is not itself a deletion target. The exact-root/descendant set cannot
  detect it. The falsification is pre-existing and reproduced twice, not a
  concurrent change. A bounded ancestry preflight plus an ordinary positive
  control can close this gap without changing schemas, receipts, operations,
  deletion targets, or accepted composition/topology contracts.
- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]], [[Current State]], `KU-048`, M4/M1
  implementation notes, MOCs, READMEs, `EXP-20260814-070/071`, and the session
  index record M4.12 `REJECT` and exact M4.13 active. No implementation,
  schema, receipt, operation, original, game asset, runtime, `.ALL`, M2, PS1,
  staged/source deletion, unmounting, or transaction behavior changes in this
  documentation-only audit.
- **Revisit when:** an intentional clean M4.13 worker commit proves the exact
  proper-parent alias refuses before mutation, ordinary cleanup remains usable,
  every mounted/ordinary/local baseline stays green, and fresh-context M4.14
  independently audits that correction.

## DEC-20260814-068 - Reject hidden stacked-alias ancestry classification

- **Context:** Independent
  [[EXP-20260814-073 - Independent M4.13 traversed-ancestry hard-truth audit]]
  audits immutable clean commit
  `4c0989ed7389556991aaa05bfe83d757b62c7d69`. The exact uncovered

  pass. Acceptance nevertheless fails in one stable stacked topology: a lower
  bind alias at the overlay-parent pathname is covered by a distinct ordinary
  top filesystem at that same pathname. Mountinfo retains both records;
  `_mount_alias_points` classifies the hidden lower record and reduces it to
  the shared pathname, so removal falsely refuses the authenticated overlay on
  the visible ordinary filesystem. Source, hidden target, visible top parent,
  and overlay snapshots remain unchanged.
- **Decision:** Return M4.14 `REJECT`, do not independently accept M4.13, and
  activate exactly M4.15. Exclude covered lower records when classifying
  proper-parent ancestry aliases, using only the already retained mountinfo
  stack/parent relationship. Require successful complete removal on the
  visible ordinary top filesystem while preserving the uncovered exact alias
  refusal and every accepted baseline.

  mountpoints; infer visibility solely from device identity; introduce general
  mount ownership/portability policy; unmount paths; or broaden removal into
  rollback/transactions.
- **Reasoning:** The safety question concerns the filesystem currently reached
  through the proper-parent pathname. A completely covered lower record is not
  that visible ancestry. Pathname-only reduction loses this distinction and
  violates the required ordinary-filesystem positive boundary. Mountinfo's
  observed same-path parent stack is the narrow evidence already retained for
  distinguishing the top record; no deletion target, schema, receipt, or
  operation change is needed.
- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]], [[Current State]], `KU-048`, M4/M1
  notes, MOCs, READMEs, `EXP-20260814-072/073`, and the session index record

  ordinary-top overlay remove completely with source and hidden target

  audits that exact correction.

## DEC-20260814-071 - Use verified Linux later-mount order and require M4.18

- **Context:** `EXP-20260814-075` rejects M4.16 because a newer uncovered bind

  IDs and `statmount` mapping to mountinfo old ID, parent, and device.

  reuse regression now refuses before traversal/unlink with source,

  survive.

  mountinfo legacy IDs only for identity, parent-stack, and metadata joins;
  use the verified explicit kernel later-mount sequence for age comparison and
  fail closed on any syscall, sequence, mapping, metadata, or table-snapshot
  disagreement. Keep M4 active on exact fresh-context M4.18.

  mounted ancestry; infer order from tuple/path/device alone; use an
  unverified syscall result; introduce broad portability/ownership policy; or
  proceed to another capability before independent review.
- **Reasoning:** The kernel's explicit later-first sequence directly answers
  the one falsified ordering question while statmount supplies an independent
  join back to the existing mountinfo evidence. Cross-checking both views
  preserves fail-closed preflight and avoids weakening M4.15 visibility. No
  schema, receipt, deletion target/order, or transaction change is needed.
- **Consequences:** [[Project Roadmap]], [[Current State]], `KU-048`, M4/M1

- **Decision:** Replace fragment-only authority alignment with the smallest
  source-aware representation for each already-claimed family. Enumerate exact
  ordered shell assignments and command/guard declarations; extract Python
  constants, schemas, formats, and operations from AST structure; require one
  generated doctor block and one exact authority row; and constrain Wine/
  README declarations to unique keyed lines or phrases in their relevant
  Markdown sections. Add the four exact M4.20 regressions first, then adjacent
  ambiguity cases only for the same already-claimed fields. Record M4.21 as
  worker-complete in
  [[EXP-20260814-080 - Unambiguous effective compatibility-authority declarations]]
  and activate only fresh-context M4.22.
- **Alternatives:** Pin whole authority-file hashes; accept fragment presence;
  parse arbitrary natural language for contradiction; build a general Bash
  security analyzer; change the statement/authority values; or advance to a
  different M4 capability before independently auditing this correction.
- **Reasoning:** Exact structured extraction rejects a competing effective
  declaration while continuing to permit the launcher's known intentional
  option and normalization assignments. AST and scoped-row/section extraction
  distinguish declarations from unrelated literals or prose without widening
  M4.21 into a general parser or policy engine.
- **Consequences:** The focused matrix expands from 18 to 36 and the canonical
  suite from 311 to 329. The exact four attacks and adjacent shell, Python,
  generated-row, Wine, and README ambiguities reject. The compatibility CLI
  retains five anchors and exactly nine asset-free reads. The statement,
  launcher, manifest implementation, three manifests, Environment/Wine facts,
  schema, receipt, operations, workflows, apply/stage/compose/remove, assets,
  runtime, M2, PS1, mount, and transaction behavior remain unchanged.
- **Revisit when:** fresh-context M4.22 returns one evidence-backed verdict on
  the immutable worker. `REJECT` selects one exact bounded correction;
  `ACCEPT` selects one remaining written M4 gate/capability or a bounded
  milestone-closure audit rather than assuming milestone completion.

## DEC-20260814-081 - Reject M4 closure and correct receipt sufficiency first

- **Context:** Exact M4.27 audits every one of the nine written M4 required

  capability requires receipts sufficient to reproduce and remove a mod.
  Current documentation explicitly says the receipt alone is insufficient.
  The install/validation/launch/removal exit gate is also not joined across one
  tool-created clean-copy lifecycle.

  [[EXP-20260814-086 - M4 v0.1 milestone-closure evidence audit]]. Activate
  only M4.28, strict reconstruction of a current existing-schema manifest from
  one byte-exact ordinary receipt so that the receipt plus exact source can
  replay and remove the same dedicated sparse overlay without the original
  manifest. Preserve receipt bytes and every existing workflow. Retain the
  separate clean-derived-copy lifecycle gate as open.
- **Alternatives:** Treat “sufficient” as receipt-plus-untracked-manifest
  despite the direct documentation contradiction; infer closure from exact
  receipts; address both gaps in one broad launch/removal automation packet;
  redesign or version the receipt; embed game bytes or outputs; add staged-tree
  deletion, packaging, rollback, or transactions.
- **Reasoning:** Eight capability rows and four exit-gate rows have traceable
  accepted evidence, current commands/tests, documentation, and explicit
  limits. Receipt sufficiency is the smallest concrete contradiction and the
  current receipt already carries normalized detail for all four operations,
  making strict reconstruction independently testable without widening the
  data or runtime surface. The lifecycle gate is broader and remains visible
  for the next reconciliation rather than being silently bundled or closed.
- **Consequences:** M4 remains Active, M2 remains Deferred, and M3 remains
  Planned. M4.27 changes documentation only and selects M4.28 with strict
  malformed/ambiguous/forged/hash/path/topology and snapshot gates. No code,
  test, receipt, manifest, compatibility, launcher, asset, runtime, local game,
  M2, PS1, mount, package, rollback, transaction, or general automation change
  is made by this audit. `make check` passes 349 tests, Vault lint 265 notes
  with zero findings, the five-anchor compatibility gate, and tracked audit 0.
- **Revisit when:** one clean M4.28 worker commit proves receipt-driven sparse
  replay and removal across schemas 1/2 and all four existing operations while
  retaining exact ordinary receipt bytes and every accepted boundary, then a
  fresh-context hard-truth audit returns one verdict. Even acceptance does not
  close the separate clean-derived-copy lifecycle gate.

## DEC-20260814-083 - Reject M4.28 at parent-component acquisition and select only M4.30

- **Context:** M4.28 checks every lexical receipt component, resolves placement,
  then opens the final file with `O_NOFOLLOW` and verifies final descriptor

  keeps already checked parents stable until acquisition completes.
- **Decision:** Reject M4.28 acceptance and keep `KU-051`, `KU-052`, and M4
  open. Select only exact M4.30: acquire and validate every parent component
  and the final receipt through stable directory handles, or the smallest
  equivalent component-by-component no-link mechanism, before replay/removal.
- **Alternatives:** Accept stable final-file bytes as sufficient despite a
  changed parent location; weaken the documented parent/placement boundary;
  redesign receipts; combine the clean-derived-copy lifecycle packet; broaden
  the correction to output/removal transactions or general filesystem policy.
- **Reasoning:** Two deterministic synthetic repetitions change one checked
  parent into a directory link immediately before final `open`, while retaining
  the same regular, single-link receipt inode and all checked metadata. Both
  calls accept and create output through the changed parent location. Final
  `O_NOFOLLOW` and final-inode checks cannot observe that parent change. All
  other evidence remains green: exact two-format mapping, 80/80 strict input
  cases, 146/208/396 tests, a 37-case removal/mount/topology selector, six local
  cycles, five pins, and nine reads.
- **Consequences:** M4.28's receipt content/mapping and ordinary behavior remain
  supported but do not close receipt sufficiency. M4.30 may change only external
  receipt acquisition, exact regressions, the resulting one pin if required,
  and documentation. Receipt bytes/schemas/operations, source/hash/guard/output
  authority, replay/removal/mount semantics, staged-tree behavior, launcher,
  assets, runtime, M2, and PS1 remain unchanged.
- **Revisit when:** one clean M4.30 worker rejects the exact parent-change replay
  and remove-receipt fixtures before mutation, proves component/final-file
  snapshots, retains every passing M4.28 gate, and is ready for a fresh-context
  audit. Do not select the clean-derived-copy lifecycle before that acceptance.

## DEC-20260814-084 - Bind external receipt acquisition to retained components and require M4.31

  receipt/output/source hashes.
- **Consequences:** Receipt and manifest bytes/schemas, four operations,

  `091a0431c24637fd8e4618bd09b208ac856c3312c3ebe5f1fb6ba7dcdff0f503`;
  five-pin membership, the statement, and nine-read scope remain exact. This

- **Reasoning:** PC static evidence independently identifies selector `0x14`'s
  terrain initialization and pickup/counter path. The 16-byte insertion plus
  556 known pointer relocations reparses, inverses exactly, boots through

  counter increments `0 -> 1`.
- **Consequences:** M3 is Active with one bounded vertical slice. M2 and M5
  remain Deferred. `KU-054` retains the exact-50 event and counter clamp, so

## DEC-20260814-108 - Close owned runtime and worker instances promptly

- **Context:** Iterative Wine/Gamescope testing left eight stale compositor/
  reaper pairs and private sinks after their individual runs. Historical M6
  verifier agents also remained listed after their bounded work had ended.
- **Decision:** Make [[Agent Operating Manual#Runtime instance lifecycle]] a
  required START HERE policy. Default to one experiment instance, retain exact
  ownership IDs, close each arm and obsolete sub-agent promptly, and finish
  with process/audio-sink audits.
- **Alternatives:** Rely on eventual process exit; kill by broad process name;
  retain idle workers in case their context becomes useful; document cleanup
  only in this session.
- **Reasoning:** Stale instances consume resources, obscure ownership, and can
  contaminate logs/input/capture. Exact-ID scoped cleanup preserves unrelated
  user processes while giving future agents an auditable terminal condition.
- **Consequences:** This session closed obsolete M6 agents and all owned
  Bugs/Wine/Gamescope/private-sink instances. Forced termination was used only
  on exact still-owned Gamescope/reaper PIDs after graceful termination did not
  close them.
- **Revisit when:** launch tooling gains an equally strict automatic ownership
  ledger and can prove cleanup without manual process/sink audits.

## DEC-20260814-109 - Complete M3.2 at the 51-of-50 bonus boundary

- **Context:** M3.1 proved 51 collectible Training records and one isolated
  new pickup, but left retained count, HUD, and completion behavior open as
  `KU-054`. The original game presents a completion/emblem event at 50.
- **Decision:** Accept M3.2 only after controlled `49 -> 50` and `50 -> 51`
  transitions, natural HUD evidence, and an exact three-byte end-tally patch
  preserve the original 50-grain milestone while visibly rendering `51/50`.
  Do not alter the `/50` denominator or emblem artwork in this packet.
- **Alternatives:** Change every literal 50; patch the unobserved cap inside
  `0x00439E90`; redefine the level goal as 51; replace or trace emblem art;
  stop at a live-memory 51 without completion evidence.
- **Reasoning:** The pickup path already retains 51 and the HUD already renders
  two decimal digits. Only the end tally clamps 51 and requires exact equality
  for completion. Raising its two cap bytes and changing `JNE` to unsigned
  `JB` is the smallest discriminated edit: values below 50 remain incomplete,
  while 50 and 51 use the original completion/emblem path.
- **Consequences:** The accepted executable is 1,069,056 bytes at SHA-256
  `8ececefbe1e5747322bd635830f4125500c087d3769094b79545bd7a7b6a4456`;
  exact inversion restores retail. `KU-054` resolves at 0–51 and `51/50`, not
  at an altered goal, new emblem, other level, or value above 51. The static
  cap in `0x00439E90` is reclassified as an unidentified mode/level behavior.
- **Revisit when:** a concrete mod request needs another collectible system,
  a different goal, emblem asset changes, or behavior above count 51.

### 2026-08-15: Separate forced prerequisite evidence from natural-death evidence

Decision: accept the EXP-116 control/candidate pair as causal evidence for the Level03 type-1 row consumer, but do not label it a natural-death receipt.

Reason: two disclosed writes reproduce the exact prerequisite state consumed by the PC `NextLife` path and yield the predicted one-byte DAT delta, while ordinary gameplay attempts did not produce a reproducible natural death. This preserves the confirmed downstream conclusion without silently closing KU-057's upstream trigger question.

Evidence: [[EXP-20260815-116-receipt]], `0x00432930`, `0x0042DDB0`, `0x0043DC88`, DAT offset `0x4A4`.

## 2026-08-15 - Separate reset-coordinate causality from checkpoint semantics

Decision: accept the Level03 type-1 row-6 edit as confirmed PC evidence for a
row-based reset-coordinate consumer after the approval-compliant rerun in
[[EXP-20260815-117 - Approved Level03 row-6 reset rerun]]. Continue to use
descriptive `type-1 row` or `reset-coordinate row` terminology. Do not rename
the table, records, or producer as checkpoints until a natural PC trigger or
equivalent independent evidence resolves [[Known Unknowns#KU-057]].

## 2026-08-15 - Supersede EXP-116 milestone acceptance with EXP-117

Any earlier Decision Log wording that treated the unapproved forced writes in

is explicitly superseded. The governance-valid basis is the fresh,
prior-authorized control/candidate rerun in
[[EXP-20260815-117 - Approved Level03 row-6 reset rerun]], which James accepted
for the bounded row-6 reset-coordinate claim only.

## DEC-20260815-113 - Adopt exact-window PC keyboard movies without an engine-frame claim

- **Context:** KU-057 needs a reproducible user Level03 route, while earlier
  experiment-local `xdotool` schedules showed useful but imperfect wall-clock
  repetition. DuckStation's debugger/input tooling is PS1-specific and is not
  an authority or implementation path for the Windows PC port.
- **Decision:** Add `scripts/input-movie.py` 1.0.0 as a PC/Wine CAP-002 worker.
  Production record may select keyboard events only on one explicitly proved
  internal Gamescope X11 `bugs.exe` window. Replay must prove executable,
  process/window ownership, common process group, stable signature, and focus;
  preserve explicit paired press/releases and a canonical schedule hash; emit
  implementation-bound JSON receipts; and emergency-release held keys on all
  exits. Keep CAP-002 partially resolved after the opening-selector validation.
- **Alternatives:** reuse DuckStation/PS1 tooling; capture `/dev/input` or the
  whole X display; keep manual text timestamps; claim monotonic offsets as
  game frames; or close CAP-002 without the intended Level03 event trace.
- **Reasoning:** `EXP-20260815-119` fixture-proves strict schema, scope,
  identity, anchor, focus-loss, and cleanup gates, then records one production
  Down/Return movie and accepts it in two fresh retail-PC process groups. Both
  replays dispatch 4/4 events, release all keys, remain below 1.8 ms maximum
  absolute drift, agree by receipt, and pass every Direct3D/no-software log
  marker. Earlier gameplay controls prove that wall-clock agreement can still
  diverge later, so the narrower claim is necessary.
- **Consequences:** [[PC input movies]] is the supported keyboard workflow and
  KU-057's tooling blocker is narrowed. No host input device, unrelated
  window, controller/gamepad, engine-frame, atomic trace/frame correlation,
  gameplay causality, checkpoint semantic, PS1 evidence, or new runtime-write
  authority is introduced. The next acceptance arm is a user-recorded
  Level03 route repeated with the read-only KU-057 trace.
- **Revisit when:** a live log-anchored Level03 route has two fresh accepted
  replay receipts, or a concrete need justifies controller input, atomic state
  correlation, or a PC-native internal-frame source.

## DEC-20260815-114 - Keep the pinned launcher immutable and add companion input controls

- **Context:** CAP-002's PC-native recorder works, but normal use required the
  user to discover and enter a Wine PID, nested X display, and X11 window ID.
  `scripts/play-pristine.sh` is an existing M4 compatibility authority with an
  exact byte pin, and the pristine PC executable must remain authoritative.
- **Decision:** Add `scripts/play-pristine-with-inputs.py` as a separate Tk
  companion. It invokes the unchanged pinned launcher in an isolated process
  session, accepts the familiar research/windowed/mute options, discovers only
  one exact owned 640x480 `bugs.exe` surface in that session, and presents
  Choose, Start Recording, Stop Recording, and Close actions. Keep recording
  delegated to the existing strict `scripts/input-movie.py` backend.
- **Alternatives:** change the pinned launcher and its M4 statement; inject a
  menu into the game or Gamescope; capture display-wide or host input; or keep
  the manual PID/display/window workflow as the only interface.
- **Reasoning:** eleven asset-free regressions prove the launcher pin, exact
  command construction, ambiguity refusal, confined create-only movie paths,
  and process-group cleanup. The separate window improves ergonomics without
  widening the trusted capture surface or changing game bytes.
- **Consequences:** the companion is the normal interactive entry point, while
  `scripts/input-movie.py` remains the evidence-producing implementation. No
  DuckStation/PS1 mechanism, host keylogger, engine-frame synchronization, or
  new runtime-write authority is introduced. `EXP-20260815-120` did not launch
  the live GUI/game, so the first user run remains a usability check.
- **Revisit when:** live target discovery fails under the supported launcher,
  the pinned launch contract intentionally changes, or a PC-native frame/input
  source is independently accepted.

## DEC-20260815-115 - Default companion routes to a shared PC log milestone

- **Context:** The first companion movie omitted renderer setup during its
  hidden three-second arm interval, and its accepted fresh replay later
  diverged at Level Select. The same live arm also exposed ambiguous
  saved-versus-next paths and group-leader-dependent terminal cleanup.
- **Decision:** Make a newly appended PC research-log line
  `--- Start LevelSelect ---` the companion's default record anchor and reuse
  the movie's recorded anchor for companion replay. Drive visible `ARMED`,
  `RECORDING NOW`, and `REPLAYING NOW` state from worker-emitted status events;
  preserve a log movie's recorded post-marker neutral interval; label manual
  delay as the weaker explicit fallback; keep last-saved and next-output paths
  separate; and clean the retained isolated process group even after its
  original leader exits.
- **Alternatives:** lengthen or hide another fixed countdown; include renderer
  selection in an elapsed-time route; infer readiness from worker process
  existence; change the pinned launcher or game bytes; or use the PS1-only
  DuckStation workflow.
- **Reasoning:** record and replay now define schedule zero from the same
  observable PC-native state transition, while the status pipe makes the
  worker's actual transition—not a UI timer—the user-visible authority. The
  23 focused fixtures cover both anchor types, status transitions, replay
  command construction, neutral-wait preservation, and post-leader cleanup.
- **Consequences:** the normal companion route begins at Level Select; the user
  still selects Hardware while the worker is armed, so renderer selection is
  setup rather than movie content. The selected Level Select marker is
  provisional until live record/replay reaches the intended game state. No
  engine-frame, gameplay-acceptance, controller, host-input, PS1, or expanded
  runtime-write claim is introduced.
- **Revisit when:** the fresh live PC route falsifies this marker, a more direct
  PC-native readiness event is established, or an accepted internal-frame
  source changes the scheduling contract.

## DEC-20260815-116 - Bound Level Select to entry and test a post-load anchor

- **Context:** The first shared-Level-Select movie corrects EXP-121's menu
  divergence: fresh replay enters Training and dispatches 300/300 inputs. The
  user nevertheless observes later gameplay desynchronization. The first
  gameplay-looking input is scheduled only 1.338 seconds after the second
  Return while the game loads and initializes the selected level.
- **Decision:** Retain `--- Start LevelSelect ---` only as a demonstrated
  menu/level-entry anchor. Do not describe one uninterrupted schedule across
  level loading as deterministic gameplay replay. Make the next bounded
  discriminator the first newly appended `NextLife` after level
  initialization, with Hardware and level selection treated as setup.
- **Alternatives:** lengthen another elapsed delay; accept complete input
  dispatch as gameplay agreement; immediately build a general multi-segment
  movie schema; or abandon the useful menu-entry result.
- **Reasoning:** `RUN-INP-REP-20260815-161221` independently passes all Hardware
  gates and loads Training, while the exact schedule and user observation
  contradict long-route synchronization. `NextLife` occurs after
  `---End InitMainGame---` in the same fresh PC log and is the nearest existing
  observable post-load boundary. A single-anchor experiment is smaller than a
  schema redesign and can falsify the load-boundary hypothesis.
- **Consequences:** companion 1.1.0 remains valid at its documented entry
  boundary but is not yet the KU-057 long-route workflow. CAP-002 remains
  partial. No engine-frame, checkpoint, load-duration, PS1, or new runtime-
  write claim is introduced.
- **Revisit when:** the `NextLife` anchored record/replay pair is observed, or
  evidence shows the first relevant divergence precedes that boundary.

## DEC-20260815-117 - Require target-stream fidelity before another anchor change

- **Context:** EXP-123's accepted 300-event replay entered Training but visibly
  desynchronized. It proposed loading phase as the leading mechanism and a
  post-load `NextLife` anchor as the next discriminator.
- **Decision:** Supersede that next-step ordering. Do not use companion 1.1.0's
  per-event `xdotool` replay as exact long-route evidence. First make target-
  window echo preserve the normalized event sequence and close-event timing;
  then A/B replay the immutable movie. Retain `NextLife` as a later phase-
  synchronization discriminator if delivery fidelity alone does not resolve
  divergence.
- **Alternatives:** implement the anchor change first; accept pre-send receipt
  drift as delivered input timing; attribute the result to general game
  nondeterminism; or silently discard close/auto-repeat-like transitions.
- **Reasoning:** `EXP-20260815-124` timestamps `NextLife` 953.942 ms before the
  first gameplay `z`, rejecting direct loading loss in the fresh replay. A
  simultaneous production observer sees 304 normalized target events for the
  300-event source, including two extra `Right` up/down pairs, and measures
  multiple 15-39 ms timing/hold changes. Source inspection shows the receipt
  timestamps immediately before a synchronous send subprocess and therefore
  cannot measure target arrival.
- **Consequences:** Level Select remains accepted for menu/Training entry only;
  CAP-002 remains partial and KU-057 remains blocked on a trustworthy route.
  No implementation change, engine-frame claim, controller scope, game write,
  PS1 authority, or exact first-visible-divergence claim is introduced.
- **Revisit when:** a corrected delivery backend passes simultaneous exact-
  window echo on this movie, followed by a fresh gameplay A/B result.

## DEC-20260815-118 - Separate exact delivery from gameplay-state agreement

- **Context:** EXP-124 proved that companion 1.1.0 transformed the target input
  stream. A persistent replay backend then delivered the immutable 300-event
  movie exactly in two fresh arms, but one returned to Level Select while the
  other remained inside Training.
- **Decision:** Accept persistent X11/XTEST plus mandatory exact-window echo as
  the CAP-002 keyboard delivery contract, while keeping deterministic gameplay
  open. Make the first fresh post-initialization `NextLife.` the companion's
  recommended/default gameplay anchor; retain Level Select as a labeled menu-
  only choice and manual delay as weaker. For every future log-anchored replay,
  capture a bounded post-anchor raw-log segment and `ROUTINE` trace, bind its
  contract into new schema-2 movies, and make replay plus receipt comparison
  reject either input-delivery or runtime-log disagreement. Retain schema-1
  playback without rewriting old movies.
- **Alternatives:** treat exact input as sufficient gameplay proof; silently
  retry until the desired outcome; convert the old movie to a new anchor using
  an inferred phase; remove `/dev/ntsync` and switch immediately to rr; or
  implement an unbounded engine-state/movie-schema redesign first.
- **Reasoning:** `RUN-INP-REP-20260815-165853` and
  `RUN-INP-REP-20260815-170119` each echo 300/300 events below 0.52 ms maximum
  target drift with identical movie/schedule/anchor and complete cleanup, yet
  their reached states disagree. `NextLife.` is independently logged after
  `---End InitMainGame---`; it removes renderer/level-selection and variable
  loading from new movie time without pretending to expose an engine frame.
  State checkpoints preserve the contradiction automatically.
- **Consequences:** the former delivery defect is fixed, but CAP-002 remains
  partial until a new user `NextLife` movie passes two fresh exact-input and
  runtime-checkpoint arms plus any task-specific state trace. Old movies remain
  replayable from their stored anchor and are not rewritten. No original,
  executable, game asset, PS1 authority, controller scope, internal-frame
  claim, game-memory write, or privileged `ntsync` action is introduced.
- **Revisit when:** a fresh `NextLife`-anchored user movie and two runtime-
  checkpointed replays agree or preserve a narrower contradiction, or a PC-
  native engine-frame/state source is independently accepted.

## DEC-20260815-119 - Add a read-only coordinate overlay for user-driven PC probes

- **Context:** Exact wall-clock keyboard delivery is accepted but gameplay
  replay remains nondeterministic. During a manual Tunnels arm, the researcher
  had to ask Codex for hidden runtime coordinates and then requested a separate
  preferably translucent coordinate window before continuing M3.5.
- **Decision:** Keep the exact PC executable and pinned launcher authoritative.
  Add a separate retail-hash/work-copy/process-group/PID-start-time-gated
  `process_vm_readv` display. Expose it through companion 1.4.0's View menu,
  button, and automatic `--coordinates` option. Use conservative raw labels,
  join overlay cleanup to the owned session, and retain a normal decorated
  window fallback.
- **Alternatives:** inject a HUD or code cave into `bugs.exe`; write game state;
  attach GDB continuously; use PS1/DuckStation state; screen-scrape coordinates;
  make deterministic movies a prerequisite for all further manual probing; or
  claim click-through/internal-frame synchronization without evidence.
- **Reasoning:** The already confirmed actor address provides direct PC-native
  coordinates. A small gather read gives the researcher immediate steering
  information without changing the behavioral reference. Exact ownership and
  PID-reuse gates prevent the helper from silently changing targets.
  `EXP-20260815-127` live-accepts process selection, read/decode, translucent
  window creation, and joined cleanup on the supported host.
- **Consequences:** Manual M3.5 work can resume with visible X/Y/Z while CAP-002
  remains partial. The overlay is a steering aid, not atomic evidence; the
  collision follow-up may race the base gather. Click-through,
  compositor-independent topmost/opacity, raw-field semantics, and engine
  frames remain outside the claim. The pinned `play-pristine.sh`, originals,
  game bytes, and M4/M6 authorities are unchanged.
- **Revisit when:** a concrete experiment needs click-through, atomic state
  capture, a different verified field set, automatic positioning against the
  host Gamescope window, or a PC-native engine-frame source.

## DEC-20260815-120 - Bind the overlay to runtime-discriminated live position

- **Context:** DEC-119 and EXP-127 used `0x0059D960` as actor XYZ based on
  earlier reset-path evidence and a selector-screen smoke. The user observed
  that none of the displayed values changed during a jump.
- **Decision:** Use `0x0059D910`/`14`/`18` as current live Flik XYZ. Record
  `0x0059D920`/`24`/`28` only as a retained preceding position and
  `0x0059D960`/`64`/`68` as an auxiliary/reset-coordinate triple. Default the
  overlay to a compact X/Y/Z-only panel inside the owned Gamescope display;
  keep conservative raw fields opt-in. Apply Gamescope's external-overlay atom
  and opacity to the mapped Tk top-level, not only Tk's child ID.
- **Alternatives:** retain the old address because it follows ordinary ground
  motion; infer vertical position from screen pixels; display all candidate
  triples without resolving them; inject a game HUD; or claim the preceding
  tuple's exact engine-frame cadence from wall-clock samples.
- **Reasoning:** In EXP-128, `0x0059D910` follows a complete visible stationary
  jump arc and later ground movement. `0x0059D920` reproduces the preceding
  distinct current tuple, while `0x0059D960` remains exactly at takeoff until
  landing. Static reads and copies at `0x004189E0`, `0x0042DDB0`, and
  `0x004AB1A0` independently support those structural roles.
- **Consequences:** EXP-127 remains valid for read-only access, identity, GUI
  creation, and cleanup, but its coordinate semantics are superseded. The
  corrected display is useful for manual M3.5 steering without changing the
  retail process. No coordinate units, internal frame, click-through, complete
  auxiliary semantics, game-memory write, or checkpoint claim is introduced.
- **Revisit when:** PC-native access traces discriminate the exact cadence and
  consumers of the preceding or auxiliary triples, or a concrete usability
  requirement needs click-through/atomic capture.

## DEC-20260815-121 - Separate spider death from the natural first-pit reset

- **Context:** KU-057's prior route hypothesis associated descriptor 571's
  retained tuple and the nearby black spider with collision result/material
  `1`. The user then identified the first grain gate, requested ten grain via
  memory editing, lowered the gate, and exercised the exposed single pit from
  two approaches while a read-only state observer ran.
- **Decision:** Accept only the bounded PC-native chain demonstrated by
  EXP-129: the first grain-gate pit produces current descriptor/value `572/1`,
  flags `0x14`, `NextLife=2`, and a nearest-row-7-like reset. Treat the
  user-confirmed spider death as a distinct descriptor/value `543/0` path.
  Retire descriptor 571 and the spider as KU-057 route targets, and do not call
  descriptor-retained coordinate tuples geometry centers without layout
  evidence. Keep KU-057 open only for natural row-6-region selection or an
  explicit milestone disposition; do not repeat the same pit for that purpose.
- **Write boundary:** The grain gate was accessed after one exact,
  user-authorized one-byte write at `0x0059D9B1`, decimal `1 -> 10`, with
  readback 10 and no other target write. This prevents calling the whole
  session pristine-state or no-write, while preserving the ordinary-input
  provenance of the subsequent spider and pit events.
- **Alternatives:** infer descriptor 571 ownership from its tuple; name the
  spider a material-1 producer despite the live value 0; generalize all value-1
  descriptors as pits; normalize the sampled `(-1,-9)` row-7 residual; or ask
  the researcher to spend more lives repeating geometry already shown to
  funnel to row 7.
- **Reasoning:** Two independent ordinary pit falls repeat the same value-1,
  `0x14`, `NextLife=2`, and post-reset endpoint sequence. The authored row-7
  X/Z is nearer than row 6 at both contact samples, and static PC code joins
  descriptor `+0x1E` through `0x004ACF90`, `0x004408EA`, `0x0041C8CC`, and
  `0x00432930`. The spider control and game log independently separate the
  other death path.
- **Consequences:** The generic natural material-1 producer question is closed
  at one Level03 pit boundary. Natural row-6 selection, type-1 checkpoint
  semantics, and behavior across other rows/levels/consumers remain open. The
  exact retail executable and assets were not edited; all owned runtime and
  sink state was closed and audited.
- **Revisit when:** a natural material-1 event occurs inside row 6's nearest-
  region, geometry-layout evidence assigns meaning to descriptor-retained
  tuples, or the M3.5 milestone explicitly disposes of its remaining row-6
  natural-selection requirement.

## DEC-20260815-122 - Bound Flik teleportation to known walkable targets

- **Context:** The user authorized Codex to take over the Tunnels probe by
  teleporting Flik and adding grain only if needed. The intended KU-057 target
  was the already-known first grain-gate pit, but the user correctly noted
  that zero-grain Flik cannot normally pass the closed gate. Two exploratory
  position-write strategies created repeating death/reset loops.
- **Decision:** Treat every teleport as write-assisted setup. For routine use,
  permit one 24-byte current+preceding XYZ syscall only to a destination
  already proven walkable, followed by readback and several observed engine
  updates. The syscall/readback is not an engine-frame or concurrency atom.
  Do not write all three triples, or equal current+preceding, at unsupported,
  below-floor, pit, or out-of-bounds points. Do not use teleportation past a
  closed gate as natural-gameplay evidence. Keep KU-057 unchanged.
- **Alternatives:** write all three triples so they remain coherent; omit only
  auxiliary and assume it stays safe; repeatedly repair auxiliary during a
  fall; increase lives; call the forced pit contact a natural death; or infer
  a safe paused/current-only technique without validating it.
- **Reasoning:** EXP-130's known walkable target was stable, but the game
  copied equal current/preceding into auxiliary about 10 ms later. It did the
  same at the unsupported pit tuple before a generic descriptor/value `548/0`,
  `NextLife=2` loop. Directly writing auxiliary also looped. Neither path
  observed material 1, and the forced closed-gate setup is not controller-
  reachable.
- **Consequences:** [[PC Flik teleport procedure]] is the durable operational
  authority. The two life-consuming loops are retained as procedure errors,
  not evidence of game design. The next M3 action remains a different natural
  row-6-region material-1 surface or explicit disposition of that narrower
  milestone requirement.
- **Revisit when:** a separate experiment validates a paused/current-only or
  guarded recovery protocol without unsafe auxiliary adoption, or a new
  naturally reachable row-6 material-1 candidate is identified.

## DEC-20260815-123 - Dispose of natural row-6 selection at the M3.5 split-evidence boundary

- **Context:** EXP-117 already causally accepts the Level03 row-6 source/
  candidate reset result, while EXP-129 supplies two ordinary descriptor-572/
  result-1 events and natural row-7 resets. M3.5 allowed either a natural
  row-6 event or an explicit disposition. Before another live arm, EXP-131
  traced the exact PC `TERRAIN.ALL` geometry and found a 16-byte row-base error
  in the earlier descriptor scanner.
- **Decision:** Mark M3.5 complete through the explicit split-evidence
  disposition. Retain the accepted causal row-6 consumer and the separately
  natural upstream/row-7 chain. Do not require another natural row-6 event for
  M3.5, and do not claim that event impossible, call the rows checkpoints, or
  generalize beyond the tested PC boundaries. Resolve KU-057 only at this
  milestone scope; move the next M3 action to final exit-gate reconciliation.
- **Correction:** Runtime descriptor base is `0x007F4590`, stride `0x30`.
  EXP-118 scanned from `0x007F45A0`, so its material/source/tag fields were
  valid but its printed X/Y/Z belonged to descriptor `index+1`.
  `(471712,64224,537728)` is descriptor 572's base, not descriptor 571's.
- **Reasoning:** Exact source records map result-1 descriptors 570–578 to nine
  X/Z regions spatially paired with all 18 type-1 rows. Descriptor 572 alone
  covers rows 6/7. Its bounds cross their nearest-row boundary, but both
  natural contacts are on the row-7 side and there is no distinct row-6-near
  result-1 descriptor. Repeating the same pit or teleporting past the closed
  gate would add risk without supplying the independent target the roadmap
  requested.
- **Alternatives:** repeat the single pit; infer controller reachability of
  its lower-Z portion from bounds alone; force a contact by teleport; keep
  M3.5 blocked indefinitely despite independent consumer and producer
  evidence; or silently preserve the old descriptor-571 coordinate label.
- **Consequences:** [[EXP-20260815-131 - Level03 collision geometry and M3.5 disposition]]
  becomes the geometry and scope authority. [[PC TERRAIN ALL collision descriptor source]]
  and its read-only parser preserve exact offsets and the Level00 variant
  contradiction. No live process, life, grain, memory write, or asset edit was
  needed. M3 itself remains Active pending exit-gate reconciliation.
- **Revisit when:** a controller-only contact selects row 6, PC evidence
  contradicts the source/runtime mapping, or a future milestone explicitly
  requires user-facing checkpoint semantics or cross-level generalization.

## DEC-20260815-124 - Close M3 at the exact four-gate evidence boundary

- **Context:** M3.1–M3.5 are individually complete, but M3 remained Active
  pending one written four-gate reconciliation. The accepted packets cover
  Training collectible/count/completion behavior, Training letter-mask/
  reward/HUD behavior, and Level03 nearest-row reset selection. CAP-002 replay
  determinism, checkpoint semantics, and broader generalization remain open
  but are not written M3 exit gates.
- **Decision:** Accept [[EXP-20260815-132 - M3 exit-gate reconciliation]] and
  mark M3 Complete at its exact documented boundaries. Treat its three
  behavior dossiers, four independent recipe families, load/play/failure/
  restoration evidence, and explicit data-versus-executable split as
  satisfying all four roadmap gates. Do not activate a successor milestone
  automatically.
- **Reproduction:** The audit rebuilt the Training 51-grain DAT, 51/50
  executable, duplicate-letter DAT, `FLIKFLIK` executable, and Level03 row-6
  DAT from exact ignored work-copy inputs. All five accepted hashes and inverse
  receipts reproduced. Their focused asset-free suites pass 25/25 under the
  canonical repository Python path; the complete repository gate passes all
  576 tests and hygiene checks.
- **Reasoning:** Keeping M3 Active would require inventing a fifth gate from
  unrelated semantic or replay unknowns. The written gates require three
  bounded PC behaviors, two independent modifications, scenario coverage, and
  a documented data/logic boundary; the evidence exceeds each threshold while
  retaining its limitations explicitly.
- **Alternatives:** repeat the Tunnels pit; use teleportation as natural
  evidence; require deterministic input replay for M3; require generic
  checkpoint or word tooling; keep the milestone open until every gameplay
  semantic is known; or activate M5 without a qualifying module.
- **Consequences:** M3 joins M0/M1/M4/M6 as Complete. M2 remains Deferred with
  its causal record-to-resource gate. M5 remains Deferred until one concrete
  module meets its contract/fixture/error/differential/value entry gate.
  CAP-002 remains a separate partial tooling capability. New gameplay work
  must begin from a concrete goal rather than by widening the closed ledger.
- **Revisit when:** new PC evidence contradicts an accepted M3 boundary, a new
  concrete mod goal requires a deliberately broader gameplay packet, or a
  specific independently implemented module satisfies the M5 entry gate.

## DEC-20260815-125 - Use a measured Python-controller and C-hot-path split

- **Context:** The researcher asked whether C would be faster than Python for
  probing and static investigation. C normally executes tight loops faster,
  but most current static work is bounded parsing/orchestration where research
  iteration, auditability, and tests dominate runtime. The project lacked one
  durable language-selection procedure.
- **Decision:** Adopt [[Python and C reverse-engineering split]]. Use Python as
  the default reference/controller. Promote only a measured hot loop or an
  intrinsically native ABI boundary to C. Keep identity, policy, lifecycle,
  schemas, receipts, and semantic interpretation in Python unless a later
  bounded packet explicitly proves a different split.
- **Required gate:** Define cadence/throughput and loss semantics, benchmark a
  representative Python reference, freeze a versioned interchange schema,
  differentially test C against Python on synthetic fixtures/read-only
  snapshots, record compiler provenance, and live-validate ownership/bounds/
  cleanup. The C helper must both preserve results and solve the measured
  bottleneck.
- **Alternatives:** write every new probe in C; keep all work in Python even
  after measured deadline loss; translate decompiler pseudocode into C; or
  choose a language from intuition without a benchmark/contract.
- **Reasoning:** This gives static work fast iteration and strong review while
  preserving access to native performance for high-rate scanning, callbacks,
  hooks, and ABI-sensitive work. It also prevents language speed from being
  mistaken for evidence authority.
- **Consequences:** Current `terrain_all.py` and coordinate overlay remain
  appropriately Python at their demonstrated bounds. A future C sampler or
  hook is neither implemented nor authorized by this decision. Normal
  debugger/write/injection approval, provenance, and cleanup rules still
  apply. Compiled helpers remain generated artifacts and do not enter Git.
- **Revisit when:** a concrete packet measures Python loss or perturbation,
  requires native ABI execution, or shows that the proposed Python/C boundary
  duplicates semantics or weakens evidence quality.

## DEC-20260815-126 - Reopen only M3 closure for Training authorization provenance

- **Context:** EXP-132 closed M3 at its worker boundary. Independent
  [[EXP-20260815-133 - Independent M3 closure authorization hard-truth audit]]
  reproduces all five exact outputs and inverses, the focused/full gates,
  static/data boundaries, and Level03 receipts. It finds that EXP-111 through
  EXP-114 disclose target-state changes and breakpoints without a durable
  record of the prior explicit approval required by the operating manual.
  EXP-116 already treats an otherwise useful unapproved write arm as
  diagnostic until an approval-compliant rerun exists.
- **Decision:** Reject independent M3 closure and return M3 to Active only for
  M3.7. Retain every exact recipe, static conclusion, Level03 result, and the
  independently supported gates two through four. Do not infer authorization
  from a historical task goal or weaken the explicit-approval rule.
- **Corrective packet:** After explicit prior approval of the exact proposed
  state changes and any breakpoint/execution control, reproduce one isolated
  appended-grain pickup and one moved duplicate-letter mask pickup in fresh
  exact Direct3D processes. Record hashes, before/after values, survival, and
  process/sink cleanup; preserve the known breakpoint-fault stop rules and all
  current validation gates. A validated durable artifact proving exact prior
  approval may replace its corresponding rerun. Then request a fresh
  independent closure audit.
- **Alternatives:** accept undocumented approval by inference; discard the
  technically valid recipes and static results; reopen Level03 or CAP-002;
  require generic gameplay tooling; launch new arms during this offline audit;
  or activate a successor milestone.
- **Reasoning:** Milestone evidence must satisfy both behavioral and governance
  provenance. The smallest correction is two authorization-compliant Training
  runtime discriminators, not a recipe, parser, semantic, or milestone-gate
  rewrite.
- **Consequences:** M3 is Active only for M3.7. M2/M5 remain Deferred; M4/M6
  remain Complete; the Python/C procedure and all target-write/lifecycle rules
  remain unchanged. No successor begins.
- **Revisit when:** the two Training behavior families have durable explicit-
  authorization provenance and a fresh independent audit accepts all four M3
  gates, or new PC evidence contradicts an otherwise retained M3 result.

## DEC-20260815-127 - Complete the M3.7 worker without self-accepting closure

- **Context:** EXP-133 rejects M3 closure only because the retained Training
  behavior receipts do not prove prior authorization for their target-state
  changes and breakpoints. The researcher directed the James
  reject/correct/audit ratchet to continue until acceptance, delegated the

  [[EXP-20260815-134 - Approval-compliant Training runtime receipts]] as the

  observation. Exclude all breakpoint, register, code, auxiliary, counter,
  mask, reward, and record writes. Keep M3 Active until a fresh independent
  audit accepts the corrected four-gate ledger.
- **Evidence:** The grain arm consumes only appended entry 62 and changes
  grain `0 -> 1`; the letter arm consumes only moved entry 63 and changes mask
  `0 -> 1`. Each retains 791 samples, zero read failures, unchanged comparison
  entries, `NextLife=0`, terminal target survival, exact hashes, and complete
  scoped cleanup.
- **Alternatives:** reconstruct approval for the historical arms; repeat the
  old GDB breakpoints; reset counters/masks/records in the fresh processes;
  accept M3 at the worker edge; reopen Level03; or weaken the authorization
  rule.
- **Consequences:** M3.7 is worker-complete and KU-059 is narrowed to the
  independent acceptance decision. M3 remains Active; M2/M5 remain Deferred;

- **Revisit when:** a fresh independent audit accepts or rejects EXP-134 and
  the complete M3 ledger, or new PC evidence contradicts either fresh arm.

## DEC-20260815-128 - Accept corrected M3 closure after the EXP-134 ratchet

- **Context:** EXP-133 independently retained M3 gates two through four but
  rejected gate one because the historical Training runtime receipts lacked
  durable prior authorization. EXP-134 follows the exact corrective packet:
  two fresh retail Direct3D processes, explicit prior authorization after the
  exact mutation proposal, one 24-byte current-plus-preceding position write
  per arm, read-only observation, and no breakpoint or state reset.
- **Decision:** Accept
  [[EXP-20260815-135 - Independent M3 ratchet acceptance audit]], resolve
  KU-059 at the exact EXP-134 two-arm boundary, and mark M3 Complete. Preserve
  EXP-133 as the historical rejection of the earlier ledger; do not treat the
  correction as retroactive approval of EXP-111 through EXP-114.
- **Evidence:** Every tracked/raw hash reproduces. Both traces contain clean
  identities, 791 samples, zero failures, one isolated record-plus-counter or
  record-plus-mask transition, `NextLife=0`, and terminal survival. The
  exercised helper rebuilds byte-for-byte from reviewed C source. All five
  recipes match accepted outputs and inverse authority; 25 focused, 9 terrain,
  and 576 complete tests pass with Vault 388/0/0, five M4 anchors, and zero
  tracked violations.
- **Limits:** Both Training arms are write-assisted and do not establish
  controller reachability. Later engine adoption into auxiliary is a runtime
  response, not a direct auxiliary write. Generic gameplay editing,
  persistence, checkpoint semantics, deterministic replay, and broader
  selector or level claims remain outside M3.
- **Alternatives:** infer retroactive approval; require every historical arm
  to be repeated; weaken the four gates; reopen Level03; treat the worker as
  self-accepting; or activate M2/M5 automatically.
- **Consequences:** M3 joins M0/M1/M4/M6 as Complete. M2 and M5 remain
  Deferred under their own entry gates; CAP-002 remains partial; the Python/C
  procedure and runtime authorization rules remain unchanged. No successor
  begins automatically.
- **Revisit when:** new PC evidence contradicts a bounded M3 result, or a new
  concrete modding/replacement goal explicitly requires a broader packet.

## DEC-20260815-129 - Keep M3 closed and expose exact level-specific grain requirements

- **Context:** After M3 closure, the researcher requested different minimum
  grain requirements for visible Tunnels and City Entrance. Static PC review
  initially reversed their routines; live internal-level identity corrected
  Tunnels to Level03/`0x00432B30` and City Entrance to
  Level04/`0x00433790`.
- **Decision:** Add a hash-locked, same-size, reversible executable builder
  for exactly nine Tunnels table values and three City Entrance indexed
  values, bounded to `0..50`. Patch both Tunnels resident/template copies and
  use the City routine's existing alignment gap for its lookup table. Preserve
  the correction history and do not claim physical transition until observed.
- **Evidence:** [[EXP-20260815-136 - Tunnels and City Entrance grain-gate requirements]],
  [[PC grain gate requirements]], and twelve focused asset-free tests. The
  `(1..9)`/`(1,2,3)` SHA-256
  `69881871c7b21d076a12585c62957efc700d33d2dfe7ea7118cf799459de284b`
  loads both relevant PC Direct3D levels with configured tables resident.
- **Alternatives:** treat the initial title association as true; edit only the
  Tunnels live table and lose values during initialization; use one City
  formula or shared minimum; activate M5; or infer gate behavior from load
  alone.
- **Consequences:** A bounded PC executable mod is available from work copies.
  M3 remains Complete; M2/M5 remain Deferred; M4/M6 remain Complete. KU-060
  records the untested physical-transition boundary.
- **Revisit when:** a modified gate is crossed at a changed threshold, another
  executable build is requested, or new PC evidence contradicts the mapping.

## DEC-20260815-130 - Require same-position physical threshold receipts

- **Context:** EXP-136 proved the edit and modified level loads but did not
  exercise a gate. The first EXP-137 City route showed a closed gate at grain
  0 from a point just outside its trigger, then opened it at grain 1 after
  moving closer. That visual pair did not isolate the count threshold at one
  position. A later candidate also showed that early Direct3D selection text
  can coexist with an eventual 320x200 software path.
- **Decision:** Accept a modified gate boundary only when below-minimum and
  exact-minimum states are captured inside the same trigger region with level,
  position, grain, gate state/animation, survival, renderer, and visible-frame
  evidence. A known walkable point reached ordinarily may be reused by a
  guarded current-plus-preceding setup, but the result remains write-assisted.
  Hardware requires the full video-memory/640x480/Direct3D-start gate now in
  [[START HERE]], not the early selection summary alone.
- **Evidence:** [[EXP-20260815-137 - Modified grain-gate threshold behavior]]
  and `dynamic/wine/EXP-20260815-137-receipt.md`. Tunnels row 3 is closed at
  3 and open at 4. City gate 0, without movement between states, is closed at
  0 and open at 1. Both retain `NextLife=0`; exact state/animation transitions
  and visible frames agree.
- **Limits:** Worker evidence covers Tunnels row 3 and City gate 0 only. It
  does not prove controller reachability, other gates, independent rows 0/4,
  changed gate numerals, persistence, or other executable builds. Independent
  James acceptance is required before KU-060 is accepted at this boundary.
- **Consequences:** The saved City approach and trigger coordinates enter the
  implementation note. The false-looking software candidate is rejected and
  the startup renderer gate is strengthened. M3 remains Complete; no milestone
  is reopened or activated.
- **Revisit when:** James rejects or accepts the worker packet, another gate is
  tested, or PC evidence contradicts either threshold transition.

EXP-138 later fulfills the independent-acceptance condition at the exact
DEC-20260815-131 boundary; this worker-edge wording remains historical.

## DEC-20260815-131 - Accept only the independently audited row-3 and gate-0 thresholds

  `(-87622,917,-182181)` as closed at 0 and open at minimum 1. Preserve both

  helper source/binary, exact profile/inverse, authorization, survival,

- **Consequences:** KU-060 resolves only for row 3/gate 0 and retains every

## DEC-20260816-132 - Retain manual hardware selection until the paired candidate passes runtime

- **Context:** Historical processes showed that the early device-selection

  but later evidence rejects generalizing either an assumed default or one
  Down to every launch. The researcher requested a native argument or other
  deterministic way to launch hardware and bypass the initial screen.

  [[EXP-20260816-143 - Automatic Direct3D startup static audit]], but do not
  implement it, change the launcher default, or call it solved. Retail
  `bugs.exe` has no native renderer option. Reject the simpler whole-chooser
  bypass because it omits required shared display setup. Keep manual hardware
  selection plus the six-marker complete-log gate authoritative at that time
  until a guarded derived-copy implementation passes fresh runtime and James
  reject/correct/accept validation.
- **Evidence:** Exact retail command-line census at `0x00403B90` and
  `0x0042C130`; probe/dispatch/chooser flow at `0x0041F990`, `0x0043DA40`,
  and `0x004A7600`; exact prospective code bytes at file `0xA6C6B`; exact
  HIGHLOW-to-ABSOLUTE relocation change at file `0xFA912`; contradictory
  retained logs `RUN-WINE-20260812-005110` and
  `RUN-WINE-20260812-231216`; independent James static acceptance of the
  corrected pair only.
- **Implementation boundary:** A future `--auto-hardware` is a project-wrapper
  option that hash/byte-guards and creates a derived work copy, applies both
  edits atomically, proves exact inverse, and records a receipt. It is not an
  argument passed to the retail executable and may never touch `originals/`.
- **Consequences:** KU-013 narrows from unknown persistence to an exact static
  candidate with runtime behavior still open. Existing launchers, binaries,
  milestones, and renderer acceptance rules remain unchanged.
- **Revisit when:** the guarded builder, repeated live Direct3D/hardware arms,
  actual-surface/playability checks, host-renderer proof where required,
  cleanup, and a fresh James ratchet all pass.

## DEC-20260816-133 - Activate M5 with one admitted module rather than a general rewrite

- **Context:** M3, M4, and M6 are complete and no successor milestone was
  automatically active. [[Current State]] required selecting a concrete goal
  and testing it against the retained M2 or M5 entry gate before substantive
  work. M5 had been deferred since the roadmap was written, with no candidate
  module ever audited against its six criteria.
- **Decision:** Activate M5 by auditing candidate modules against
  [[Project Roadmap#Module entry gate]] and admitting exactly one — the
  type-1 reset placement selector — as work packet M5.1. Keep the milestone's
  admitted-module table explicit so activation never becomes a licence for a
  general engine rewrite. Reject the `.ANM` first-level format for now (no
  bounded contract, `KU-022`) and anything on the M2 dependency path (its
  causal gate is still open under `KU-023`). Language stays Python under
  [[Python and C reverse-engineering split]]: the module is offline, low
  rate, and has no measured hot loop.
- **Evidence:** [[EXP-20260816-153 - M5 entry gate and type-1 reset placement resolver]].
  Contract derived from disassembly of the exact retail hash at
  `0x00432930` (file `0x031D30`), byte-identical variant `0x00437A20` (file
  `0x036E20`), and loader slot writes `0x0044BCA9`/`0x0044BC8B` (file
  `0x04B0A9`/`0x04B08B`). Differentially validated against
  `EXP-20260815-116`, `-117`, `-129`, and `-130`, composed with the
  independently accepted height model in `EXP-20260816-141`/`-142`.
- **Implementation boundary:** `reimplementation/ablpc/type1_reset.py` reads
  level data and reports; it never writes game bytes, ships no assets, and is
  not a level editor. It refuses the three cases where the original is
  undefined or mislayed rather than emulating them. The confirmed reset role
  stays Level03/Tunnels; type 1 keeps its address-based description and is
  still not named a respawn or checkpoint table.
- **Consequences:** M5 is Active and owns the next work packet; M2 stays
  deferred and M3/M4/M6 stay complete and untouched. No M4 authority, pin,
  statement, launcher, or manifest schema changed. New unknowns `KU-062` and
  `KU-063` are recorded rather than assumed away.
- **Revisit when:** a fresh-context M5.2 audit accepts or rejects the module,
  or a second candidate is proposed — in which case it passes the same
  six-criterion audit before any code is written.

## DEC-20260816-135 - Admit the grain-gate opening resolver as M5's second module

> [!correction] Rejected worker decision

> is DEC-136 below.

  evaluator of the Tunnels and City Entrance grain-gate opening decision.

  original runtime is its decisive first failed criterion, while the remaining

  Tunnels `0x00432B30`, comparison `0x00432F0C`, opening write
  `0x00432F6A`, coupling `0x00432F85`/`0x00432F9D`; City Entrance
  `0x00433790`, comparison `0x004337D6`, opening write `0x004337EF`;
  proximity helper `0x00416580`; accepted EXP-137/138/144/145/148/149
  receipts; exact retail SHA-256
  `97c95ba5572dce637f6ea6325dbfff0c9a2d002a30d3dc88f39e0f558a27b878`.
- **Proposed boundary:** Inputs are explicit family, signed 32-bit
  Flik X/Z, unsigned grain, current gate state, requirement, center, radius,
  and family-specific record/slot metadata. Outputs are eligibility, ordered
  direct opening writes—including City index-0 `[0x00599C69] = 1`—downstream
  decay/state/slot-delta metadata, and a non-opening reason. Byte semantics,
  callee effects, messages, audio, renderer effects, controller reachability,

  remain complete.
  The later coverage preference Actors/AI → Input → Event/scripting is
  recorded but does not supersede M5.6.1 or waive any PC-native entry gate.
