## DEC-20260816-137 - Implement M5.7 as an ordered opening evaluator with downstream metadata

- **Context:** DEC-136 and EXP-169 independently admit one second M5 module at
  the twelve-mechanism Tunnels/City boundary. The native Tunnels loop is
  ordered (`0x00432EEA-0x00433043`), so row-0 coupling changes row 4 before
  its later visit while reverse row-4 coupling occurs after row 0's visit.
  City writes state `0x20` at `0x004337EF`, index 0 additionally writes byte
  1 to address `0x00599C69` at `0x00433807`, and the same call then begins its
  positive-state/slot-delta countdown at `0x0043383B-0x00433879`.
- **Worker decision:** implement Tunnels as one pure nine-row ordered opening
  pass and City as one pure call-site opening evaluation. Return direct writes
  separately from downstream decay/countdown metadata. Apply only direct
  opening writes to private result state; do not execute animation, decay,
  helper side effects, messages/audio, rendering, persistence, control, or
  other update logic. Preserve address-named unknown Tunnels `+0x04` and keep
  `0x00599C69` semantic-Unknown.
- **Arithmetic:** reproduce low signed 32-bit `sub`, arithmetic `sar 8`,
  wrapping `imul`/`add`, and final signed `<` from `0x00416580` and
  `0x00432F33-0x00432F4F`. Preserve native refusal order: Tunnels checks grain
  before opening proximity; City checks proximity before grain. Guard the
  admitted authored requirements to `0..50` and grain to an unsigned byte.
- **Interface decision:** keep the offline low-rate reference in Python under
  [[Python and C reverse-engineering split]]. Require explicit exact records
  or call-site metadata; expose strict asset-free JSON reporting that writes
  only stdout. The existing executable threshold builder remains unchanged.
- **Evidence:** [[EXP-20260816-170 - M5.7 grain-gate opening resolver]],
  `static/exports/BIN-97c95ba5572dce63/M5-grain-gate-resolver-static-trace.md`,
  exact retail SHA-256
  `97c95ba5572dce637f6ea6325dbfff0c9a2d002a30d3dc88f39e0f558a27b878`,
  and accepted EXP-137/138/139/144/145/148/149 receipts.
- **Evidence limit:** row 1's original runtime receipt proves only “opens
  above minimum”; row 4 is runtime-nonseparable because row 0 opens it. Tests
  may exercise the native model for both but must not manufacture the absent
  original-runtime claims. EXP-146 remains direct/static scope only.
- **Consequences:** the corrected M5.7 worker is independently accepted at
  exact commit `dfd3af31c845939aa972babfd0f44a60e54b6487` by
  [[EXP-20260816-171 - Independent M5.8 grain-gate resolver audits]]. KU-065
  qualifies every full-suite pass; KU-067 remains open; no broader trigger
  family, downstream execution, runtime replacement, or missing receipt is
  admitted.
- **First audit correction:** fresh-context M5.8 rejected exact worker commit
  `efad1c255dcfda31e225c7fdf62608bc59f63e5a` because default `json.loads`
  accepted duplicate object keys last-value-wins, contradicting the strict-
  JSON interface decision. The bounded correction installs a duplicate-
  detecting object-pairs hook at every nesting level and tests request, gate,
  record, stdin, and named-path behavior. This changes no evaluator arithmetic,
  refusal order, coupling, direct writes, downstream metadata, receipt claim,
  threshold tooling, or evidence boundary. A wholly fresh re-audit accepts the
  exact correction after literal and escaped all-level adversarial cases,
  resolver 38/38, combined 64/64, and a KU-065-qualified canonical 686/686.
- **Revisit when:** a separately admitted integration packet supplies new
  evidence, a six-criterion third module is proposed, or new PC evidence
  contradicts the model.

## DEC-20260817-138 - Close the event census unaccepted, with a stated confidence limit

- **Context:** The PC event subsystem census (`EXP-20260817-185`) went through six independent hard-truth audits. Every round confirmed the model — table geometry, record layout, the seven extents, the two decoded records, disjointness from the creature scripts — and every round found fresh defects in the prose around it: 12, 10, 16, 12, 15, 14. The defects were never in the conclusions; they were miscited addresses, incomplete enumerations, classification slips, and corrections that failed to reach every carrier. Round 6's own convergence judgement was that the model converged at round 1 and the document was not converging under that process.
- **Decision:** Stop the audit loop at round 6 and close the packet **unaccepted**, with the Event/scripting row remaining at **Bounded** and an explicit confidence limit recorded on the export: its conclusions are well-supported, but its individual addresses and enumerations have a demonstrated defect rate and must be re-verified against the binary before being relied on.
- **Alternatives:** Continue auditing to acceptance; downgrade the row to Touched; delete the packet and re-derive it in one pass.
- **Reasoning:** No milestone gates on this row, and the Vault codifies no acceptance requirement — the audit loop is a working convention. Six independent re-derivations is unusually strong support for the conclusions, so downgrading or discarding would lose real evidence. What the audit history genuinely establishes is a *detail-level* defect rate, and the honest response to that is to state it rather than to keep grinding or to hide it behind an acceptance stamp.
- **Consequences:** Any future work that depends on a specific address, offset, or enumeration from this packet must re-derive it from the binary first. The conclusions may be cited normally. `KU-071` remains open. The export is the normative statement; the nine Vault carriers point to it rather than paraphrase it, so future corrections have one place to land.
- **Revisit when:** Someone needs a detail from this packet for a milestone-gating claim, or runtime evidence becomes available that would move the row toward Mapped.

## DEC-20260817-139 - Implement the live-session recipe as tools with an ownership ledger

- **Context:** The complete live-session procedure lived only as prose in [[PC autonomous game control]]. Each runtime arm re-derived the nested display, re-proved identity, re-drove the chooser, navigated the carousel by screenshot, and cleaned up by hand. Three findings — XTEST-only delivery, ~180 ms held presses, focus-gated logic — had been learned and then re-learned, the second time at the cost of a wrong-level arm (`EXP-20260816-156`). Separately, the `EXP-20260816-163` closure audit found 32 `winedevice.exe` processes it could neither attribute nor terminate, because no launch had ever recorded what it owned.
- **Decision:** Implement the recipe as `scripts/game-session.py`, `game-memory.py`, `send-keys.py`, and `wait-for.py` over a shared `scripts/session_lib.py`, and make every launch write an ownership ledger at `dynamic/wine/RUN-SESSION-<stamp>/session.json`. Session identity is `(pid, process group, PID start ticks)`; cleanup signals only recorded identities and skips a PID whose start ticks have changed. Ship it **fixture-validated and explicitly not live-validated**, with live validation as CAP-010's acceptance test.
- **Alternatives:** Keep the prose recipe and rely on agents following it; write a single monolithic driver script; wait and build it only alongside a live arm.
- **Reasoning:** Prose could not stop the failures it described — the wrong-level arm happened with the recipe already written. Encoding the three findings in code makes them enforceable, and the ledger converts "terminate only what you recorded" from an instruction into a check. Splitting into four verbs keeps each tool's authority separate: reads are ordinary, writes are gated. Shipping without live validation is honest and useful: the fixture proves the logic, and the live arm is a separate authorized runtime action that should not be bundled into a tooling packet.
- **Consequences:** Nothing in [[Session control plane]] may be cited as runtime evidence until CAP-010's acceptance test passes. The manual procedure in [[PC autonomous game control]] remains authoritative in the meantime and is not deleted. Two new inferences are now load-bearing enough to track: `KU-074` (carousel counter live semantics) and `KU-075` (software-arm 320x200 log wording).
- **Revisit when:** the first authorized live arm either confirms the tools or contradicts any transcribed address, marker, or timing.

## DEC-20260817-140 - Gate memory writes on authorization, a compare-before-write guard, and a receipt

- **Context:** [[Agent Operating Manual]] requires explicit approval for memory writes, but enforcement was procedural. In practice each packet rebuilt a throwaway C or Python helper (`teleport_flik_keep_anchor.c`, `grain_gate_probe.c`, and about eight others under ignored `work/copies/`), each re-deriving identity proof and syscall framing, with the mandatory preflight in [[PC Flik teleport procedure]] enforced only by prose. `EXP-20260815-130` showed the cost of getting a write wrong: an unsupported destination made the engine adopt a bad reset anchor and repeat `NextLife=2` until Game Over.
- **Decision:** Give writes exactly one implementation, `game-memory.py poke` / `teleport`, gated on a mandatory `--authorize` reference recorded in the receipt, a mandatory `--expect-current` compare-before-write guard (with `--no-expect` as a disclosed, receipt-recorded escape), post-write readback verification, and a receipt under `dynamic/wine/RUN-POKE-<stamp>/` written on refusals as well as successes. `teleport` additionally requires `--destination-evidence` citing why the destination is already proven walkable, writes current and preceding only, and reports a nonzero `NextLife` after settling as tool-negative.
- **Alternatives:** Keep per-experiment helpers; add a write verb to the GDB harness; require a human to run every write by hand.
- **Reasoning:** The gates in the teleport procedure are good; they were simply unenforceable as prose, and rewriting them per packet is how they get skipped. Keeping writes out of `gdb-harness.py` preserves that tool's audited `write_verbs: []` contract, which several accepted experiments depend on. A refusal receipt matters as much as a success receipt: a guard mismatch is evidence that the address moved.
- **Consequences:** Building this surfaced a defect in the procedure's own wording. Current XYZ (`0x0059D910/14/18`) and preceding XYZ (`0x0059D920/24/28`) are **not contiguous** — an unidentified dword sits at `0x0059D91C`. "One 24-byte `process_vm_writev`" must therefore mean one call carrying two 12-byte vectors; a 24-byte contiguous range would overwrite that unknown dword and misalign the second triple. [[PC Flik teleport procedure]] is corrected accordingly.
- **Revisit when:** a write class appears that the guard cannot express, or live use shows the settle check is too weak to catch a reset loop.

## DEC-20260817-141 - Treat the startup reading set as a measured budget

- **Context:** [[START HERE]] mandates seven documents before substantive work. Measured on 2026-08-17, they totalled ~272,994 estimated tokens — more than a 200K context window. No agent could comply as written, and the fresh-context audit loop paid the cost once per round. [[Agent Operating Manual]] already required that [[Current State]] stay concise with chronology in session notes; the note had instead accumulated 55 dated packet narratives in place.
- **Decision:** Make the budget a measured, enforced quantity rather than an impression. `scripts/vault-lint.py --budget-only` reports per-note and total token estimates and warns past 40K/120K. `scripts/briefing.py` generates a ~4,600-token briefing whose safety rules are non-elidable and whose unparseable sections fail loudly. [[Current State]]'s chronology moved **verbatim** to [[Current State Archive]].
- **Alternatives:** Shorten the mandate by dropping documents; summarize the large notes in place; leave it and let agents skim unpredictably.
- **Reasoning:** Dropping documents loses evidence and rewriting them in place destroys the audit trail — the move is verbatim precisely so nothing is lost or reworded. A generated briefing is safe to trust only if it cannot silently omit a safety rule or invent a fact, so the rules are non-elidable and an unparseable section is reported as `UNPARSED` rather than guessed.
- **Consequences:** The briefing is generated and **not authoritative**; any claim relied upon must be verified in the cited note. The mandate itself is unchanged: this makes compliance possible, it does not license skipping. [[Project Roadmap]] (~92K) and [[Decision Log]] (~78K, this note included) remain over budget — CAP-016's residual, and the same archive split applies.
- **Revisit when:** the mandated total fits the session budget, or the mandate is changed by a separate decision.

## DEC-20260817-142 - Automate the gates the audit loop was enforcing by hand

- **Context:** The repository had zero CI and zero git hooks; `make check` ran only when someone remembered. KU-065's first four failures were lost because pytest output was piped through `grep`, discarding the `FAILED` line. Separately, the dominant audit-rejection cause was never a wrong model: the creature census was rejected eight times and the event census six, with postmortems recording the same defect repeatedly — a correction landing "only at the first occurrence" of a wording.
- **Decision:** Add `.github/workflows/check.yml` (raw unfiltered pytest output retained as an artifact, failures extracted from the retained file afterwards, plus a job repeating the historically flaky node 50 times), `scripts/git-hooks/pre-commit` with `make install-hooks`, `pytest.ini` scoping collection to `tests/`, `tests/test_repo_hygiene.py`, and `scripts/claim-sweep.py` whose `pair --old --new` verb reports a tree carrying both wordings as tool-negative.
- **Alternatives:** Rely on the audit loop; add CI without log retention; write a linter that understands claims semantically.
- **Reasoning:** Every one of these targets a specific recorded failure rather than a hypothetical one. Log retention is the direct fix for the way KU-065 evaded identification for four occurrences. `claim-sweep pair` does not need to understand a claim to catch the actual defect — the defect is always a tree in a mixed state, which is mechanically detectable. `pytest.ini` fixes a genuine hazard: a leftover Wine prefix at `tmp/wine-smoke-2370022/dosdevices/z: -> /` made a bare `pytest` follow that symlink out of the repository and never terminate.
- **Consequences:** Run `claim-sweep pair` after every correction and before handing a packet to an audit. CI covers the synthetic suite only; runtime and live-game work is explicitly out of scope and stays with the authorized-arm procedure.
- **Revisit when:** an audit round is lost to a defect class these gates do not cover.

## DEC-20260817-143 - Save states are a same-process region checkpoint, not CRIU

- **Context:** The largest remaining per-arm cost is that every level-N experiment repeats boot + chooser + carousel, because `Escape` does not exit a level and there is no in-level return to the selector (`CAP-011`, [[PC autonomous game control]]). The user asked for save-and-reload. The two candidate mechanisms were CRIU over the Wine process tree and an in-process memory checkpoint.
- **Decision:** Build the in-process memory checkpoint as `game-memory.py snapshot`/`restore`, capturing curated game-data regions — by default the single writable `.data` section `0x004C1000`–`0x00864000`, which holds every identified global and is neither code nor stack — with a `SIGSTOP` freeze for atomicity, restored into the **same process** by default. Do **not** build CRIU now; instead ship `criu-feasibility.py`, a read-only survey that reports whether the durable path is viable.
- **Alternatives:** CRIU dump/restore of the whole Wine tree; an 86Box/VM whole-machine snapshot; a Wine-prefix clone.
- **Reasoning:** The feasibility survey run against the live game shows CRIU is blocked by an open `/dev/ntsync` fd and `ntsync` anonymous inodes — the same driver that already defeats `rr` (`KU-028`) — plus a GPU DRM fd and open sockets (35 hard fds), and needs `CAP_CHECKPOINT_RESTORE` or root. The in-process path sidesteps all of that by moving memory only, needs no root, and builds on the already-validated `process_vm_readv`/`writev` foundation. It genuinely removes the repeated boot cost within one session, which is the actual complaint. The honest limitation is that it is memory-only and same-process, so it is scoped and named as a state checkpoint, not a savestate.
- **Consequences:** Restore is an authorized memory write with a receipt, and it refuses a different process, a level change, or an executable mismatch by default. It does not survive a crash, a close, or a relaunch — that remains CRIU or a VM snapshot, both recorded under `CAP-011` with the ntsync blocker as the gating fact (shared with `KU-028`). Snapshots hold game bytes and are written only to git-ignored `work/snapshots/`.
- **Revisit when:** an experiment needs a checkpoint that survives a relaunch (pursue the `KU-028` ntsync remedy plus CRIU, or the `CAP-009` VM route), or the same-process checkpoint is shown at runtime to be insufficient for the state an experiment depends on.

## DEC-20260817-144 - Save states get in-game hotkeys grabbed on the nested display

- **Context:** The CLI `snapshot`/`restore` model works but the user wants an emulator-style experience: press a key while playing to save (with a filename popup) and load, scoped to the game rather than the whole Linux desktop. The game is `bugs.exe` under Wine, not an emulator, so there is no built-in savestate menu to extend.
- **Decision:** Add `scripts/savestate-hotkeys.py`, a daemon that grabs a few configurable hotkeys with `XGrabKey` **on the game's nested X display only** (never host `:0`), shows a Tk filename popup on save and a picker on load, and drives the existing live-validated `game-memory.py snapshot`/`restore` underneath. Defaults F5 save / F9 load / F6 quicksave / F7 quickload / F12 quit; any key is remappable (e.g. `--save-key F1`).
- **Alternatives:** A system-wide hotkey daemon (rejected: the user explicitly wanted it scoped to the game, not environment-wide); the Tk companion window with buttons (rejected earlier by the user); binding into the game itself (not possible without patching the binary); an actual emulator with native savestates (86Box, not the Wine-first runtime).
- **Reasoning:** `XGrabKey` on the nested display is exactly "scoped to the game" — the grab only affects that X server, which is the game's environment. It reuses the validated snapshot/restore path and its write-gating rather than introducing a second write surface; the human keypress is the load authorization, and the receipt records that. A live probe confirmed the grab works on the running game's nested display `:2` without conflict, so the mechanism is feasible, not speculative.
- **Consequences:** These are still same-process checkpoints (they do not survive a relaunch; that is CRIU, blocked by ntsync, CAP-011). The `game-memory` backend is live-validated but the interactive keypress→popup→save/load flow is not yet — that is the daemon's acceptance test and needs a human at the keyboard. Saves are game bytes and go only to git-ignored `work/snapshots/`.
- **Revisit when:** the interactive flow is live-run and either confirmed or found to misbehave under Gamescope (e.g. the game's own keyboard grab interfering), or the user wants persistent named-slot management beyond a flat directory.

## DEC-20260817-145 - Deterministic replay via libTAS + single-threaded PCem, not rr

- **Context:** The project needed deterministic, reproducible replay of the PC game for investigation and regression testing — record a run, replay it identically, rewind/inspect. The `rr` route was pursued to exhaustion (see [[2026-08-17 2200 - Claude - rr Wine dead-end and PCem-libTAS pivot]]): `rr` genuinely works on the game's code (deterministic replay + reverse execution proven), but only under classic 32-bit Wine, and the game **crashes/hangs loading a level** under the classic Wine 9.0 that `rr` requires (`KU-076`, CONVPSXTPAGE8 corrupt-pointer). The Wine that records it crashes it; the Wine that runs it (11, new-WoW64) cannot be recorded. The user confirmed the PC is the definitive modding target — easier and far less memory-constrained than PS1 — so PS1 is a bounded reference only, not a substitute.
- **Decision:** Split the project into two environments. **Fedora + Wine** stays primary for asset/exe analysis, mod development, and fast iteration. Deterministic replay moves to a **libTAS + pinned single-threaded PCem (TASVideos "PCem 17+st") + Windows 95** harness running the real PC executable. **86Box** is an optional later experiment (better debugger) only if it can be shown deterministic; never a dependency. Abandon `rr`-on-Wine.
- **Alternatives:** Build classic 32-bit Wine 11 from source to make `rr` work (large, uncertain, poor ergonomics — rr crawls on this game, 8.3 GB/60 s traces); move dynamic work to PS1/DuckStation (rejected — different architecture, harder modding, 2 MB memory ceiling, not the target); accept no deterministic replay on PC.
- **Reasoning:** PCem-st runs *real Win95 + the real PC binary*, so there are no Wine-compat issues (the CONVPSXTPAGE8 crash is Wine-version-specific and likely absent on real Win95 — to be confirmed) and findings apply to the PC port directly. libTAS provides exactly the missing capability — frame-perfect record/replay/rerecord/savestates — via a documented, community-proven TAS route, unlike the uncharted rr-on-Wine path. It keeps every PC advantage while adding determinism. The critical path (determinism) is de-risked with the proven single-threaded PCem fork before exploring the 86Box debugger enhancement.
- **Consequences:** The first milestone is narrow and gated: boot the game under Win95/PCem-st, reproduce the CONVPSXTPAGE8 load (also the discriminating test for `KU-076`), capture a level-start savestate, and demonstrate repeated synchronized playback. Determinism is verified progressively (understood globals → full `.data` hash → diff-and-classify → normalised fingerprint), which doubles as RE evidence about which bytes are meaningful game state. Requires user-supplied Win95 media and the TASVideos ROMs (`ga686bx/6BX.F2a`, `voodoo3_3000/3k12sd.rom`, `awe32.raw`). Host `ntsync` is restored and the `rr-abl` container torn down.
- **Revisit when:** PCem-st is shown NOT to replay this game deterministically (then reconsider 86Box-st or the classic-Wine-11 build), or a need arises that only rr's instruction-level reverse execution can serve.

## DEC-20260817-146 - Guest memory read/write/watch is a hard prerequisite, tested before the determinism build

- **Context:** `DEC-20260817-145` scoped the deterministic harness with an acceptance test that only checked *determinism* (repeated synchronized replay, `.data` fingerprint). The user correctly identified the gap: libTAS sees the **PCem host process** memory, not the guest game's Win95 virtual addresses. The mapped range `0x004C1000-0x00864000` and every address in the `game-memory` map are **guest virtual addresses**; they are not directly visible from libTAS on the Fedora side. Replayability without guest-memory access is only reproducible gameplay video, not a reverse-engineering tool.
- **Decision:** The harness must satisfy **two** gates, and **Gate 2 is tested first** because it is the riskier, more novel one:
  - **Gate 1 — determinism:** same savestate + same movie -> same guest state, repeatedly (progressive `.data` fingerprint per `DEC-20260817-145`).
  - **Gate 2 — guest address-level control:** from the host/emulator, read a known guest game address (e.g. `0x00861C84` level, `0x0059D910` Flik position), write it, and ideally watch it (break on write). If Gate 2 cannot be made to work cleanly, the PCem/libTAS approach loses most of its RE value and must be reconsidered.
- **Mechanism decision:** guest memory access is done **host-side via the emulator**, not via an in-guest agent. An in-guest probe (`ABLProbe.exe`-style) runs guest code during replay and perturbs the deterministic timeline; it also cannot run while libTAS is frozen. The emulator instead already holds guest RAM as a host buffer; resolve the game's virtual address to a guest physical page by walking its page tables (via `CR3`), then read/write that buffer. Reads are passive (timeline untouched); writes are the intended experiment; watchpoints are natural for an emulator (it emulates the memory bus). Win9x is pre-ASLR so the game's load base and layout are stable, making the translation tractable. The existing `game-memory` address map carries over unchanged — it only needs a new backend (emulator read instead of `process_vm_readv`).
- **Sequencing change:** 86Box moves from "optional later" to "test early." It is already installed and has a real debugger (PCem's is weaker). Prove Gate 2 in 86Box **before** building single-threaded PCem for determinism. If 86Box passes Gate 2 *and* can be pinned deterministic, it can be the RE workstation with PCem-st as the authoritative deterministic reference; if it only does one, split the roles.
- **Open uncertainty:** whether any available emulator debugger (86Box's, PCem's) cleanly handles a Win9x *process virtual* address (with page-table translation) rather than only physical/linear addresses is **unproven**. That is exactly why Gate 2 is an early hard gate, not an assumption.
- **Revisit when:** Gate 2 is demonstrated (then proceed to Gate 1 / determinism), or shown impractical on the available emulators (then reconsider the whole harness, possibly a custom emulator memory hook or a non-deterministic 86Box debugging arm paired with PCem-st replay).

## DEC-20260817-147 - Build a GDBSTUB-enabled 86Box to prove Gate 2 memory access

- **Context:** `DEC-20260817-146` made guest read/write/watch a hard prerequisite (Gate 2) and elevated 86Box to test it early, on the assumption that 86Box's GDB stub already gives virtual-address access. A ChatGPT refinement pushed to test the **existing** stub before writing any custom CR3 walker, and to split Gate 2 into 2A (read/write/watch known ABL linear addresses), 2B (establish the ABL address-space/CR3 context), and 2C (verify debugger reads don't break deterministic replay). Direct inspection of the installed build and the 86Box source was done to check these assumptions. Evidence: [[2026-08-17 2330 - Claude - 86Box GDB stub feasibility for guest memory access]], `KU-077`.
- **Decision:** Build 86Box from source with **`-D GDBSTUB=ON`** (host once deps are installed, or in a throwaway podman container matching the rr-container pattern) and use it to prove Gate 2 via the 2A/2B/2C procedure. Depend **only on linear/virtual addresses through the stub**; do **not** hand-write a CR3/page-table read-walker — the stub is the walker. The existing `game-memory` address map carries over; only the backend changes (stub `m`/`M`/`Z` packets instead of `process_vm_readv`).
- **Alternatives:** Use the installed b9001 AppImage (rejected — it has **no** stub: empty `strings | grep -i gdb`, no `--help` option, and the stub is CMake-gated `GDBSTUB` default OFF); write our own emulator memory hook / CR3 walker now (rejected — premature; the stub already does linear translation and watchpoints); start with PCem-st's debugger (deferred — determinism host, debugger capability unverified; prove the mechanism on 86Box's confirmed-capable stub first); an in-guest agent (already rejected in DEC-146 — perturbs determinism).
- **Reasoning (all confirmed from `src/gdbstub.c` + `src/CMakeLists.txt`, master):** the stub reads/writes **linear** addresses via `readmembl`/`writemem*l` under `cpl_override=1` with `ABRT_PF` checks (full MMU translation through the active CR3), and supports Z2/Z3/Z4 write/read/access watchpoints. So "break inside known ABL code, then read `0x00861C84`/`0x0059D910` as linear addresses" is a real capability once the game's CR3 is the active context — which is exactly why the experiment must set a **code** breakpoint in bugs.exe first (2B). The installed build lacks the stub: the **ELF string scan** is the direct evidence (the `GDB Stub: Listening on port` log string / `gdbstub` symbols are absent), corroborated by stable builds shipping `GDBSTUB=OFF`. The `--help` absence is only *consistent*, not proof — the stub auto-inits from `machine_init()` (port 12345) with no CLI flag. A source build is unavoidable.
- **Consequences:** Gate 2A cannot run until a GDBSTUB build exists; this reintroduces a from-source build (86Box deps) as a prerequisite, obtainable without host sudo via a container. The stub's read path uses the normal MMU accessors that **can set page-table A/D bits** (it does not use a no-side-effect translate), so 2C must measure whether inspection perturbs savestate→replay identity; if it does, patch the stub read to a no-A/D/no-abort path. 86Box is proven **best-debugger** but is **not** a TAS/deterministic emulator, so its role is to prove the address-access **mechanism** decoupled from determinism (Gate 1 stays PCem-st or an equivalent). No-ASLR guarantees stable **virtual** addresses but **not** identical virtual→physical mapping run to run — depending only on linear addresses via the stub sidesteps that entirely. Finally, `-D GDBSTUB=ON` forces `DYNAREC=OFF` (disables `NEW_DYNAREC` off ARM64), so this artifact is an **interpreter-core debugger/RE build only** — not evidence about 86Box determinism or timing relative to the normal dynarec build; Gate 1 stays a separate build/emulator question.
- **Revisit when:** Gate 2A/2B/2C pass on the GDBSTUB build (then decide the determinism pairing — deterministic 86Box config vs PCem-st vs split roles, and settle Gate 1), or the stub proves unable to reach the game's linear space cleanly at a code breakpoint (then reconsider a custom emulator memory hook or a different emulator).

## DEC-20260818-148 - Stand up the game on real Win95 in 86Box; deploy the game to the HDD, not the emulated CD

- **Context:** Gate 2 needs the real PC executable running on real Win95 inside 86Box (`DEC-20260817-146/147`). The user supplied Win95 OSR2 media + an OEM key and chose an unattended/headless build. Building it surfaced a chain of concrete blockers, each resolved with evidence in [[2026-08-18 0330 - Claude - Win95 and A Bug's Life installed in 86Box]]; the reusable recipe is [[86Box Win95 RE harness]].
- **Decision:** Adopt a **headless 86Box + Win95 OSR2** machine (i430VX / Pentium-200 / S3 Trio64V2 / SB16, `work/86box/config/86box.cfg`) as the Gate-2 runtime substrate, driven in a `podman` container via Xvfb + ImageMagick screenshots + `xdotool` XTEST. **Deploy A Bug's Life to the hard disk at `C:\PC\BUGS\`** (extract-to-HDD from the ISO) rather than running it from the emulated CD, and **remove the guest real-mode CD driver** so the game loads its assets from C:. Keep the Win95 disk image portable so the same disk boots under both the stock (dynarec) build for play and the gdbstub build for the probe.
- **Alternatives considered:** run the game from the emulated ATAPI CD (rejected — 86Box regression [#5432](https://github.com/86Box/86Box/issues/5432): real-mode CD driver returns CDR101 on builds ≥6700, and Win95's protected-mode CD driver did not auto-attach); Win95's protected-mode CD via Device-Manager detection (deferred — GUI-heavy, and HDD deployment removes the need); install via the game's InstallShield `SETUP.EXE` (rejected — needs the working CD and mouse; extract-to-HDD is deterministic and host-controlled); PCem-st as the substrate (deferred to the determinism question, `DEC-20260817-145/146`).
- **Reasoning:** HDD deployment sidesteps the CD regression entirely and is fully host-controlled (mtools). Placing the game at `C:\PC\BUGS\` reproduces the known-good Wine "installed layout"; with no CD-ROM drive present, the game's content-root selector falls back to the local layout and passes the `cd.txt` gate — the exact behaviour documented in [[EXP-20260811-004 - Wine launch and CD sentinel gate]] / [[FUNC-97C95BA5572D-00403B90]], now confirmed on real Win95. The disk being build-independent lets the fast dynarec build install/play while the interpreter-core gdbstub build does the probe.
- **Consequences:** The game runs to its level-select menu, proving the substrate works — but this is the environment, not the result: **Gate 2 (the GDB probe) is not yet run**, and **KU-076** (the CONVPSXTPAGE8 level load) is **untested** because the menu is point-and-click (headless keyboard cannot enter a level). Music/cutscenes were trimmed for size; add them back from `work/86box/gamefiles/held/` if a run needs them. The build process is a driven session, not a script (`KU-078`); the golden artifacts are `work/86box/disk/win95_c_clean.img` and `win95_c_game.img`. The OEM key lives only in git-ignored `work/` and the guest registry, never in a tracked note.
- **Revisit when:** Gate 2 passes or fails on this substrate (then decide whether 86Box or PCem-st is the keeper and settle Gate 1), or the trimmed asset set proves insufficient to reach gameplay (restore `AUDIO`/`TEMP`).

## DEC-20260818-149 - Build the runtime "flight recorder" as emulator-side C instrumentation, not gdb watchpoints

- **Context:** [[EXP-20260818-001 - Watchpoint path and first forensic tuple for Flik position]] proved the GDB-stub watchpoint *primitive* works (it fired at writer `0x004ab889` and produced a CR3-verified forensic tuple for Flik`s position), but that the **gdb/RSP transport is too fragile for continuous capture** — the stub retains watchpoints across unclean exits ("too many"), emits a malformed `T` stop-reply that crashes gdb ≥15, and its `continue` is non-deterministically async in batch mode. The goal (from the adopted "flight-recorder" framing, and `KU-077`) is *forensic replay* — record write-evidence during one playthrough and analyse it offline — as the tractable first capability, separate from *experimental replay* (restore + modify + continue), which needs a machine checkpoint and is deferred. DuckStation/PS1 is explicitly out of scope (cross-port, non-authoritative).
- **Decision:** Build the recorder as a **C modification to the v6 `GDBSTUB=ON` 86Box build** (source + build tree already in container `abl-86box-v6`). It hooks the `mem.c` guest-write accessors — where the address (guest-**linear**) and the value are both in scope, alongside the existing `GDBSTUB_MEM_ACCESS` macro — filters by a page/range window and by CR3 (`extern uint32_t cr3`; game = `0x00454000`), and appends a record `{cycle (tsc), cr3, eip (cpu_state.oldpc), addr, new-value, width}` to an in-emulator **circular buffer** (the *old* value is derived offline as the previous same-`addr` record, not stored — this is the design vision; the shipped Phase 0/1 record is exactly this). A **`SIGUSR1`** to the 86Box process (works headless and native, no guest cooperation) dumps the trace ring today; the full "bookmark package" (trace window + guest RAM + registers + a frame) is the **Phase 1b/3 target**, not yet shipped. **Offline Python** (`scripts/ablrec-*.py`, run locally by Claude — no upload) answers forensic queries ("who wrote X near marker N", "value timeline of Y", "group writes by EIP", auto-cross-ref EIP → the Ghidra export). Determinism is **not** required for the forensic layer and is not pursued.
- **Why C-side, not gdb:** the v6 stub build runs the **interpreter** (`exec386`; the recompiler is compiled but a `GDBSTUB` build never selects it — `KU-077`), so **every** guest write goes through the `mem.c` C accessors carrying `GDBSTUB_MEM_ACCESS` — no dynarec fast-path can bypass the hook. The interpreter runs the game playably (~118 Hz), and the hot-path cost is the page-bitmap test 86Box already pays for `GDBSTUB_MEM_ACCESS` plus, for watched pages, a value read and a ring append. `cpu_state.oldpc` gives the **writer`s** EIP directly (better than the watchpoint`s trap-after PC).
- **Alternatives considered:** gdb/RSP hardware watchpoints for capture (rejected — proven too fragile/non-deterministic in EXP-20260818-001); a patched multi-client/non-leaking stub with a corrected stop-reply (deferred — larger stub surgery than a direct write hook, and still slower than in-process capture); libTAS-over-Wine deterministic replay (rejected for this — Wine`s WoW64 + old-Wine level-load crash, and its "rewind" is state-load+fast-forward, not instruction-level); DuckStation/PS1 (out of scope — cross-port). Experimental replay via 86Box full-machine savestates is deferred to Phase 3 pending a test that they reliably restore the running game (never assumed — `KU-077`/`DEC-20260817-147` note the stub build has no *conventional* savestate workflow proven).
- **Phasing:** **Phase 0 (go/no-go) — PASSED** ([[EXP-20260818-002 - Flight-recorder Phase 0 write-trace]], patch `tools/86box/patches/86box-v6.0-ablrec.patch`): the C write-trace reliably reproduces `0x004ab889 → 0x0059D910` (53 hits in one short movement) without gdb, and captures the full writer set for Flik`s position struct at ~119 Hz. Load-bearing detail discovered here: 86Box`s fast-write cache (`writelookup2`) lets repeated writes bypass the accessors, so the hook must keep the target page uncached — done by marking it in `gdbstub_watch_pages` and gating `addwritelookup`. **Phase 1:** all widths, CR3 + configurable range filter, full `dump_mark()` bookmark package (trace + RAM + regs + frame). **Phase 2:** offline query tools. **Phase 3 (deferred):** full-machine checkpoint at a mark for restore→modify→continue.
- **Consequences:** the recorder binary is a **debugger/RE build**, not a determinism datapoint (like the stub build), and diverges from upstream 86Box — kept as a tracked source patch + the built binary under git-ignored `work/86box/`, never committed as a binary. It shares the fragile stub`s limitation that it only runs the interpreter core; it is not a fidelity claim about dynarec timing. Open questions carried into Phase 0: confirm `cpu_state.oldpc` yields the writer (not trap-after); confirm the `cr3` global is correct at the write site; measure FPS with the hook active; choose RAM-snapshot scope (full 64 MB vs. the game `.data` linear range via a CR3 walk).
- **Revisit when:** Phase 0 fails to reproduce `0x004ab889` reliably (then reconsider the hook site or a patched stub), or the interpreter-only constraint blocks a needed measurement, or experimental replay becomes required (promote Phase 3 and test 86Box savestates).

## DEC-20260818-150 - Use Anthill Level01 as the primary R2 static-mesh causal target

- **Decision:** run the first authorized type-`0x41` Gate C experiment on the
  exact Anthill Level01 candidate in
  [[EXP-20260818-008 - R2a Anthill static mesh causal candidate]]. Retain the
  Level03 candidate from EXP-007 as a fallback or later replication.
- **Reason:** R0 and R1 are corpus/executable-wide and do not need repeating.
  Anthill already has a retail hash, a repeatable hardware-Direct3D start
  capture, and recognizable visible floor. Level01 primary record 81 supplies
  a zero-rotation, near-zero translated-component, one-face vertex edit whose
  object and geometry are each referenced once, making the causal outcome more
  observable and localized than the earlier Level03 selection.
- **Boundary:** this is target selection, not runtime evidence. No derived DAT
  was written or launched. Exact screen pixels remain unpredicted because the
  archived control frame does not preserve a camera transform; R2b must use
  matched A1/B/A2 captures and keep an unlocalized no-change result ambiguous.
- **Evidence:** [[EXP-20260818-006 - R0 the static mesh format and where visible ground lives]],
  [[EXP-20260818-007 - R1 static mesh consumer, round trip, and causal candidate]],
  [[EXP-20260818-008 - R2a Anthill static mesh causal candidate]], and
  [[EXP-20260812-013 - Level 01 target rebind no visible start effect]].

## DEC-20260819-151 - Keep R2 open after the inconclusive Anthill run

- **Context:** [[EXP-20260819-001 - R2b Anthill static mesh causal runtime]]
  completed valid retail/candidate/retail hardware-Direct3D arms for the
  EXP-008 one-byte candidate. Only `108 / 307200` pixels were byte-identical
  across all 30 control frames, far below the frozen `0.75` agreement gate,
  and there was no independent proof that the edited face was in view.
- **Decision:** keep R1 complete and R2 open. Do not promote the candidate to
  visible ground, reject it, or record a no-effect result. The next causal
  attempt must be a newly committed and preregistered stable-camera or
  steady-state packet that establishes control agreement and independently
  localizes the edited face in view before effect classification.
- **Reason:** the experiment's ordered acceptance rule stops at the failed
  control-agreement gate. Corresponding identical frames and a zero candidate
  mask cannot override that gate or establish visibility of the edited face.
- **Consequences:** retain the valid lifecycle and substitution mechanics, the
  exact one-byte candidate, and all fail-closed renderer/identity/level/audit
  gates. Change only the observation design needed to make a causal comparison
  interpretable.
- **Evidence:** [[EXP-20260818-008 - R2a Anthill static mesh causal candidate]],
  [[EXP-20260819-001 - R2b Anthill static mesh causal runtime]], and `KU-079`.

## DEC-20260819-152 - Split R2c localization from the one-byte candidate

- **Context:** R2b's all-frame control mask crossed a deterministic global
  fade-in and did not independently establish that face 8 was visible. The
  retained `t0 + 3.300 s` retail frame is fully bright and exact across both
  controls, while R1 identifies face 8's exact four index words.
- **Decision:** R2c is two separately accepted causal stages. First calibrate a
  retail-only, exact-PID `SIGSTOP` freeze at `t0 + 3.300 s`. Then localize face
  8 through a guarded orthogonal topology edit that degenerates only its index
  tuple. Commit and obtain James acceptance of the localizer result/evidence
  packet, never the derived DAT, before preparing or launching any successor to
  the one-byte vertex candidate.
- **Reason:** a frozen compositor duplicate is not an independent sample, a
  camera matrix does not prove occlusion-visible pixels, and an adaptive ROI
  derived and consumed inside one mutable candidate packet would permit
  post-hoc interpretation. The topology edit supplies a distinct causal form
  without relying on unknown coordinate, material, colour, or UV semantics.
- **Consequences:** the first requested runtime authorization covers only F0
  and, conditionally, C1/localizer/C2, including exact-PID process signals and

## DEC-20260819-153 - Require the committed compositor-backed RC0 executor

- **Context:** RC0-F0-004 showed that interactive ImageMagick capture can
  consume the chooser timeout. `capture-fixture-002` then produced stable
  `640x480` files through X11-root `ffmpeg`, but RC0-F0-005 proved those files
  and its live frame were the same uniform black root image (PNG SHA-256
  `724319c36...c6931`), not game pixels. Ad hoc receipt and marker guards also
  allowed RC0-F0-005 to reach timeout before Return, although no experiment
  input was sent and cleanup completed safely.
- **Decision:** R2c renderer calibration must run only through the committed
  `scripts/r2c-rc0-executor.py`. Capture uses the previously live-validated
  asynchronous game-surface backend, `GAMESCOPE_WAYLAND_DISPLAY=gamescope-0
  gamescopectl screenshot <canonical-absolute-new-path>`, followed by file-size
  stability and strict decoded-image gates. The executor owns stage/evidence
  freshness, retail/prefix/CD/PID/window guards, three stable known-chooser
  captures, the no-direction Return, terminal-log classification, receipts,
  fail-closed cleanup, and retail-link restoration.
- **Historical operational identity:** that executor revision accepted no path
  overrides and pinned the retail source plus the stage/pending identities for
  setup 006. DEC-20260819-154 supersedes those consumed identities. Baseline
  `R:` had to initially resolve to retail. It bounded the
  compositor subprocess independently of the asynchronous file wait, uses the
  direct guarded XTEST focus/Return sequence rather than fixture-only
  `send-keys.py`, rehashes before input, and requires a final exact live
  PID/window/mapping/hash guard before hardware handoff.
- **Reason:** command success and `640x480` dimensions do not establish game
  pixels. A chooser allowlist is presence-only and cannot identify renderer
  selection. Likewise, the preliminary `Native software render mode is FALSE`,
  `RenderMode set to Direct3d`, and `Screen mode 640x480x16` lines are not
  terminal creation evidence; classification still requires the full hardware
  or software terminal set after Return.
- **Consequences:** `ffmpeg` X11-root and both ImageMagick paths are prohibited
  for R2c. Unknown, black, wrong-size, or unstable images stop before input.
  Mixed/incomplete renderer logs stop after Return. A complete hardware result
  alone remains live in the same process for F0; all other outcomes close and
  audit. F0 signals/reads and localizer creation remain blocked until that
  handoff passes the existing gates.
- **Evidence:** [[EXP-20260819-002 - R2c Anthill face-8 freeze and localization protocol]],
  `RC0-F0-005-black-root-capture`, `RUN-FRZ-20260812-183223`,
  `scripts/r2c-rc0-executor.py`, and `tests/test_r2c_rc0.py`.

## DEC-20260819-154 - Settle the R2c chooser before a deadline-bounded triplet

- **Context:** RC0-F0-006 passed the retail, prefix, CD, PID, and window gates,

  executor now pins `RC0-F0-retail-007` and pending `RC0-F0-007`. After the
  exact CD/window gate it waits a fixed `4.000 s` with no input while polling
  the same process/window and pre-Return log. It then captures the three-frame
  triplet inside one absolute `45.000 s` launch-to-Return deadline.

  immediately after capture and retain each image's dimensions, PNG/decoded-
  RGB hashes, non-uniform state, black-root state, and allowlist result before

  terminal-log, or deadline evidence stops before Return.

  still presenting startup/fade pixels. A fixed settle removes post-hoc frame
  selection, while the shared deadline prevents the 60-second chooser timeout

- **Consequences:** RC0-F0-006 remains an unconsumed pre-input rejection. RC0-
  F0-007 is the only next RC0 identity. F0 signals/reads and localizer creation

## DEC-163 - Require a two-process exact sampled-phase gate before another face-8 localizer

**Date:** 2026-08-19
**Status:** Superseded after rejected D1 runtime

**Decision:** Replace adaptive or endpoint-based phase matching with the fixed control-only procedure in [[EXP-20260819-003 - R2d Anthill sampled phase calibration]]. D1 and D2 must be separate unmodified-Level01 processes. Both must independently exhibit the same smallest exact decoded-RGB period on the preregistered 121-point, 0.200-second lattice with at least two complete repeats. D1 alone defines a fixed nuisance mask; D2 may validate or reject it but never expand it.

**Why:** R2b/R2c retained exact cross-arm endpoints but did not establish a repeated same-process live sequence. A stopped duplicate is technical stability, not independent phase replication. Combining a localizer with post-hoc period or mask selection would allow the treatment arm to choose its own comparison gate.

**Consequences:** Failure or disagreement publishes no accepted mask. The result is scoped to sampled recurrence, not a continuous animation period. The runner stops before any localizer/candidate arm. D1/D2 and any later C/L/L/C packet require separate explicit runtime authorization and James acceptance boundaries.

**Audit refinement:** An arm's clean runtime lifecycle is provisional rather than aggregate acceptance. Independent receipt validation must pass, D1/D2 `(PID, start_ticks)` process identities must differ, and the complete pair/mask gate must accept before either arm remains in the accepted namespace. PGID remains separately retained launch provenance and cannot make a matching PID/start identity independent. Any clean aggregate failure quarantines provisional arms as rejected; cleanup ambiguity keeps the affected arm and aggregate pending.

**Runtime outcome:** D1 captured all 121 frames and rejected on lower-right crop drift. All full-frame RGB hashes were unique and no exact sampled period existed. D2 remained unrun. DEC-164 replaces the cycle model.

## DEC-164 - Replace exact-cycle calibration with a finite stable-background mask

**Date:** 2026-08-19
**Status:** Superseded by DEC-165 after consumed D1 runtime rejection

**Decision:** Use [[EXP-20260819-004 - R2e Anthill stable-background calibration]]. Fresh D1 marks the union of exact RGB inequality against D1 frame zero across a fixed 121-frame/24-second grid, dilates it by a clipped Chebyshev radius of three, and must remain at or below 35% coverage. Fresh D2 may not expand that mask and must match D1 frame zero at every unmasked pixel across all 121 samples.

**Why:** The consumed EXP-003 D1 evidence proves no exact period on that grid and proves `lower-right-structure` is temporally variable. A finite exclusion mask directly tests the stable pixels needed by a later comparator without inventing a cycle.

**Evidence-selection disclosure:** The 35% cap was chosen after the consumed diagnostic measured 29.5941% dilation. It is preregistered for fresh arms but has no independent support from that rejected control.

**Runtime disposition:** The user authorized EXP-004. D1 captured all 121 frames and rejected because the fixed full-frame F0 identity was phase-sensitive while all three camera-control crops remained exact. D2 was not run; no accepted mask was published. DEC-165 supersedes this design.

**Consequences:** The retained pending/classified provenance and camera crops carry forward, but exact full-frame phase equality does not. Localizer, visibility, terrain, causality, retail confirmation, and exhaustive nuisance claims remain outside scope and separately authorized.

## DEC-165 - Remove full-frame phase equality from stable-background calibration

**Date:** 2026-08-19
**Status:** Superseded by DEC-166 after consumed aggregate rejection

**Context:** Authorized EXP-004 D1 captured its complete 121-frame grid with exact renderer, Level01, timing, lifecycle, and three camera-control crops, then rejected because frame zero did not equal a full-frame F0 image from a different live-animation phase. Its diagnostic measured 78,593 raw and 92,630 radius-three changing pixels (`30.152994791666665%`) and no exact period. D2 was not run.

**Decision:** Supersede EXP-004 with [[EXP-20260819-005 - R2f Anthill phase-agnostic stable-background calibration]]. Fresh D1's actual first sampled frame is the fixed reference for its complete-grid union. Preserve the three exact camera-control crop gates, radius-three dilation, 35% cap, and independent D2 zero-outside-mask holdout. Remove only predetermined full-frame phase equality. D2 cannot select a reference or expand D1's mask.

**Boundary:** The consumed EXP-004 authorization does not transfer. EXP-005 is offline and requires fresh explicit authorization. Success would establish only a finite exact-view/build sampled stable-background region, not a cycle, exhaustive nuisance model, face visibility, terrain identity, causality, or retail behavior. Any later C/L/L/C intervention remains separately preregistered and authorized.

**Evidence:** [[EXP-20260819-004 - R2e Anthill stable-background calibration]], [[EXP-20260819-005 - R2f Anthill phase-agnostic stable-background calibration]], `D1-SB-AH-001`, and diagnostic receipt SHA-256 `4d54e41a99f729f042dd75bc00c1a4ada464acab9b85ab7938deb4acfe706fef`.

## DEC-169 - Require independent L1/L2 replication for the face-8 localizer

**Date:** 2026-08-19

**Status:** Completed with inconclusive runtime result; successor sequencing superseded by DEC-170

**Decision:** The offline design required [[EXP-20260819-007 - R2i Anthill replicated face-8 localizer]] to use exact `C1 -> L1 -> L2 -> C2` order. A single localizer process was insufficient. L1 and L2 had to be independently published from separate retail reads, have identical exact three-byte face-8 mutations, and reproduce every outside-mask RGB value at corresponding timestamps.

**Acceptance boundary:** Controls exactly equal D1 outside the accepted mask. A positive localizer requires one at-least-25-pixel static core, exactly one at-least-25-pixel union component, at least 75% dominant-component coverage, and at most 5% full-frame union coverage. Inside-mask changes are nondiscriminating. Any failure is inconclusive, never no-effect evidence.

**Consequence:** A pass would have been scoped to a reproducible localized rendered change in the derived build/view/grid. Ground, terrain, collision, material, coordinates, retail behavior, and the one-byte candidate remained open. The required authorization was later granted and consumed; the run was procedurally valid but scientifically inconclusive, and DEC-170 now governs successor sequencing.

## DEC-170 - Do not adapt the nuisance mask from EXP-007 localizer arms

**Date:** 2026-08-19
**Status:** accepted

**Decision:** Preserve [[EXP-20260819-007 - R2i Anthill replicated face-8 localizer]] as procedurally valid but scientifically inconclusive. Do not widen or select a mask from its L1/L2 arms and do not reinterpret its zero static core as no effect. A successor mask proposal must be an offline control-only diagnostic, then pass a fresh independently authorized control holdout before any separately authorized face-8 localizer successor.

**Evidence:** Aggregate result SHA-256 `723e03ed...a309`; 251 control outside-mask mismatches; 228 L1/L2 disagreements; zero intersection/static core. The exact L2 frame-35 228-pixel difference is reproduced by C2 frame 35, proving that observation is nuisance. James independently reproduced and accepted this inconclusive interpretation.

**Consequences:** R2 remains open. Face-8 visibility and terrain identity remain unconfirmed. The one-byte candidate remains unauthorized. The accepted static L1/L2 pair may be retained, but none of the four quarantined runtime arms may be promoted or reused.

## DEC-171 - Require a fresh D4 holdout for the control-trained mask

**Date:** 2026-08-19
**Status:** accepted

**Decision:** Permit EXP-007 C1/C2 controls, but not L1/L2, to train one bounded offline successor nuisance-mask proposal. Apply exactly `old_mask OR chebyshev_dilate(raw_new, radius=3)` so the old radius-three mask is not dilated twice. Do not accept or use the proposal for another localizer until one fresh, separately authorized D4 control produces exact zero differences outside it.

**Evidence:** [[EXP-20260819-008 - R2j Anthill control-trained successor mask]]; diagnostic SHA-256 `83931a8a...9a5`; 249 unique control pixels; 93,445-pixel proposal; independent James reproduction and offline-only acceptance.

**Consequences:** EXP-007 remains inconclusive. D4 cannot adapt or expand the mask and does not automatically authorize a localizer. Even a D4 pass is one finite derived-build/view/grid holdout, not exhaustive nuisance coverage, terrain/ground identification, face-8 causality, retail confirmation, or collision evidence.

### DEC-171 outcome update - EXP-009 D4 passed

EXP-009 satisfied DEC-171's fresh-holdout condition: 121/121 frames and zero outside-mask observations/unique pixels, result `bf2b4625...69d8`. This promotes the EXP-008 proposal only to one finite derived-build/view/grid nuisance reference. It does not authorize a localizer or establish terrain, visibility, causality, no effect, retail behavior, or collision.

## DEC-172 - Separate the accepted D4 mask check from a fresh replicated face-8 intervention

- **Date:** 2026-08-20
- **Status:** executed; procedural packet accepted, scientific result inconclusive
- **Decision:** Use the exact accepted D4 result and fixed 93,445-pixel mask as immutable predecessor evidence, but do not reuse any EXP-007 production path, arm, process, DAT, or comparison. Freeze a new C1/L1/L2/C2 packet with two independently read and guarded localizer copies. Treat inside-mask changes and zero outside-mask change as inconclusive.
- **Why:** D4 supports finite nuisance-mask adequacy only. It does not retroactively rescue the inconclusive EXP-007 arms. Independent localizer derivation and replication are required before any build-scoped face-visibility claim.
- **Boundary:** Even a positive result is limited to the exact face-8 mutation, auto-hardware executable hash, frozen title view, and sampled grid. Terrain/ground, collision, materials, and retail behavior remain unconfirmed.
- **Outcome:** The pair and all four arm lifecycles passed, but L1/L2 had `20,634,078` outside-mask RGB disagreements, zero all-frame intersection/core, three large components, and `68.896%` union coverage. Quarantine the arms as scientifically inconclusive; do not call this a face-visibility or no-effect result and do not authorize the candidate.
- **Next decision:** Permit only offline cross-time correspondence and camera-safe state diagnostics over the retained L1/L2 frames and unchanged mask. A future runtime design requires a unique reproducible correspondence or newly preregistered read-only state anchors.
- **Evidence:** [[EXP-20260819-009 - R2k Anthill D4 control-trained mask holdout]], [[EXP-20260820-001 - R2l fresh replicated face8 localizer]].

## DEC-173 - Wall-clock correspondence must close exactly before another face-8 runtime design

- **Date:** 2026-08-20
- **Status:** executed; accepted diagnostic, no unique fixed lag
- **Decision:** Compare all retained R2l L1/L2 frame pairs outside the unchanged mask and on the three frozen camera-safe crops. Evaluate only fixed integer lags `[-60,60]`; require zero eligible-pixel disagreement for every overlapping pair, exact control crop hashes, temporal variability in both arms, at least 61 overlaps, and exactly one passing lag.
- **Why:** R2l controls closed but the byte-identical localizer arms diverged broadly. Approximate nearest-frame matching could select a favorable phase after the fact and cannot support a new causal protocol.
- **Boundary:** Minima, ties, mutual-nearest pairs, and heatmaps are retained but nonselecting. A unique lag is discovery evidence only and authorizes nothing. No unique lag forbids repeating this wall-clock protocol; future runtime then requires independently justified read-only camera/player-state anchors.
- **Outcome:** No lag passed. L1 had no eligible-pixel temporal variability, L2 did, and all 121 lags failed exact outside-mask/crop/control/variability gates. The 1,815 exact individual pairs remain nonselecting. Close fixed wall-clock alignment as nonviable and move only to offline/static PC state-read candidate justification.
- **Evidence:** [[EXP-20260820-001 - R2l fresh replicated face8 localizer]], [[EXP-20260820-002 - R2m offline cross-time correspondence diagnostic]].

## DEC-175 - Require a successful DirectDraw present inside each R2 state sample

- **Date:** 2026-08-20
- **Status:** frozen offline design; runtime unauthorized
- **Decision:** Do not treat `0x0040FFE3`, `0x00405F90`, or EndScene return as
  sufficient frame evidence. Admit a completed R2o sample only when the same
  guarded normal `0x0040FFC0` invocation and TID passes both R2n phase PCs,
  successful EndScene, exactly one successful fullscreen Flip or windowed Blt
  return, and then `0x0040FFE3`.
- **Why:** ShowBack can skip presentation, restore a lost surface, or fail, and
  its caller ignores the result. Static CPU order cannot establish when the
  compositor exposes a surface.
- **Protocol:** Use two fresh unmodified controls, exactly three complete
  invocations plus one terminal completion-to-next-phase screenshot-equality
  check per process, exact
  ordinal state/image equality, stopped triplicates, and a nonstatic image
  discriminator. Nearest-state selection and adaptive extension are forbidden.
- **Boundary:** Hardware breakpoints and debug-register changes are execution
  perturbations; software breakpoints are prohibited code writes. The executor
  requires offline implementation, tests, commit, James acceptance, and then
  fresh explicit runtime authorization. No terrain, face, material, collision,
  causal, no-effect, compositor-general, or retail claim follows.
- **Evidence:** [[EXP-20260820-004 - R2o completed-present state calibration protocol]],
  `static/exports/BIN-97c95ba5572dce63/R2O-completed-present-boundary.md`.

## DEC-180 - Preserve exact R2o task-census disagreement before verdict

- **Date:** 2026-08-20
- **Status:** completed and consumed; superseded by DEC-181
- **Decision:** Consume `D1-AH-004` and aggregate `completed-present-state-calibration-004` as a truthful first-trap task-census rejection. For the later `-005` identities, retain the exact no-churn gate and atomically journal every census attempt with its phase, sorted expected/observed `(TID,start_ticks)` pairs, additions, removals, reused-TID start changes, attached/stopped sets, timestamps, and terminal state before applying a verdict. Bind every census expected/attached identity to the immutable open task list. Authenticate the complete rejected journal prefix against the exact R2o action/value, raw-stop, slot/DR6/active-debug, chained debug-state, and exact baseline-restore grammar. In accepted evidence, bind open, every stop-all, and every capture-before/after census one-to-one to its physically adjacent journal action ID with no duplicate, swapped, or unused receipt. After any mismatch allow only exact debugger/process cleanup.
- **Evidence-integrity correction:** Require the completed ptrace-open list to
  equal the immutable sorted `(TID,start_ticks)` census, not merely the TID
  set. Validate stop payloads through the supported recorded register layout,
  derive their size as three native words, and require one layout throughout
  the packet. The retained `-004` stop is `x86_64` with 24 bytes.
- **Raw-evidence correction:** Require each breakpoint receipt's raw
  `wait_status` to decode as stopped `SIGTRAP` with `ptrace_event = 0`, and
  compare the completed ptrace-open task list in its recorded order to the
  immutable sorted census. Do not normalize a reordered receipt into validity.
- **Why:** `-004` reached the first exact `0x0040FFC0` hardware trap after arming local DR0 on its initial 16 tasks, but its immediate stop-all census differed. The prior evidence recorded the rejection without a durable independently recomputable census delta or a terminal failed stop-all action. The evidence does not identify the task-lifecycle cause, so dynamically enrolling, ignoring, or removing tasks would weaken the frozen coherence rule.
- **Boundary:** Census enumeration/receipt ambiguity remains pending. A changed census rejects before TID selection, further breakpoint programming, R2o reads, captures, or ordinary resume. The `-005` D1 condition was met; DEC-181 now governs the separate task-lifecycle diagnostic. This decision authorizes no runtime, process-memory read, debugger action, DAT/localizer/candidate, or terrain/collision/material/visibility/causal claim.
- **Evidence:** [[EXP-20260820-004 - R2o completed-present state calibration protocol]], rejected D1 disposition SHA-256 `553bf96d...f0ff`, debugger cleanup SHA-256 `cc5bfe04...0431`, rejected aggregate disposition SHA-256 `df90a7e1...9f38`.

  `(1834786,30382246)`, not the frozen Xwayland `(1834812,30382270)`, so the
  isolated fixture did not reproduce production socket provenance. The
  retained post-close audit has only a raw census because Make stderr made its
  combined stream unparsable.

  task-lifecycle samples and supports no lifecycle, collision, terrain,
  material, visibility, causality, or retail-behavior claim. Seven live
  Gamescope/Wine processes and `ablpc_mute_1834769` remain recorded but

  corrected passive successor needs a new explicit authorization and frozen

## DEC-190 - Require exact integer domains and complete X peer credentials

- **Date:** 2026-08-21
- **Status:** rejected offline candidate; superseded by DEC-191
- **Decision:** Accept James's **REJECT** of exact `932627d` / tree `4742aa21`.
  Require strict JSON integer types with positive/nonnegative domains throughout
  P0A identities and chronology. Cross-bind both X receipts by their complete
  PID/UID/GID/start tuple, require the exact owned Gamescope peer not to predate
  the launcher, and prohibit launcher/game/peer PID collisions. Validate sample
  timing before direct enumeration-drift classification and apply the same
  numeric strictness to metadata brackets, navigation, raw/path assets, audit
  counts, windows, and title input.
- **Why:** Python treats booleans as integers. Internally consistent scientific
  and rejected packets could therefore forge boolean PIDs, start ticks,
  connection fields, audit return codes/times, or inner counts. Separately,
  two sockets could claim different peer credentials, or one impossible PID
  could simultaneously be launcher, game, and Gamescope. These are evidence-
  identity failures, not harmless representation differences.
- **Boundary:** This is offline validator/executor hardening only. P0A-AH-001
  remains consumed; there is no P0B or successor identity. No game input,
  process-memory access, close, signal, screenshot, `R:` restoration, evidence
  publication, or launch is authorized. The earlier seven-process audit is
  historical; the `2026-08-21T05:35:13-04:00` read-only census records three
  unattributed helpers plus sink and no open session, with prior game/Xwayland
  absent and no causal claim.
- **Evidence:** [[EXP-20260820-005 - R2p task lifecycle diagnostic]], 269/269
  analyzer/lifecycle/packet regressions, separate native X pass (270 focused
  total), 1914/1914 full tests, zero tracked-file violations, and
  collector/validator SHA-256 `71f9099a...28bd`.

  replaced that route's direct input with a persistent held-state delivery and

  passive grid removes that procedural defect without process-memory access.

  violations; corrected James re-audit remains.

  There is no signal fallback, post-collection xdotool fallback, save state,

  tests with zero tracked-file violations. This accepts the offline pre-runtime

## DEC-196 - Require published-envelope and register-transition provenance in R2q

- **Date:** 2026-08-22
- **Status:** fulfilled by James-accepted `-011` offline basis; no runtime authority
- **Decision:** Accept James's rejections of `-005`, `-007`, and `-008` and require
  the active correction to derive
  every claimed control-state transition from authenticated bytes and exact
  before/desired/readback identities. D1 and D2 share one exclusive attempt
  identity and one P0A/R/P0B invariant; D2 authenticates D1's accepted root
  from a canonical tree manifest bound to the published file and parent.
  Publish one acyclic control envelope whose manifest and receipt-map bytes are
  the same bytes reread by the two-control aggregate. Any active debugger,
  close, audit, mapping, publication, or classification uncertainty is
  pending. Failed actions retain and validate their successful receipt prefix,
  name the exact failed-next receipt, and reduce nonaccepted cleanup through
  the same authenticated launch/attach/register state as accepted controls.
  D2 must reread the canonical D1 tree manifest, both controls need distinct
  post-D2 reread receipts, and publication binds source/destination parent and
  stat chronology. The entire NUL-separated command line is strict UTF-8, and
  aggregate rejection exposes only `DesignError`.
- **Why:** `-005` could pass while the log named the wrong level, D2 was
  unreachable after D1, renamed destinations differed from temporary files,
  breakpoint PCs were assertions, EndScene lacked a second continue/wait, and
  cleanup uncertainty became a scientific rejection. Those are evidence-
  provenance failures, not wording defects.
- **Boundary:** The design remains an argument-free offline publisher with no
  runtime entry point. It authorizes no launch, `/proc`, ptrace, memory,
  signal, input, screenshot, drive mapping, or scientific conclusion. Even
  James acceptance would establish only readiness to request a separate exact
  runtime authorization.
- **Evidence:** [[EXP-20260820-005 - R2p task lifecycle diagnostic]], James
  rejection of exact `814ada4054be6fa345bff39cc83d37eea6887381` / tree
  `b79e88587a5bbc99337eeab3d45e540bbbc58eaf`; superseded pre-audit `-006`
